workflow: let's have a doc for each and every day moving forward. i used to do this in my notes and i think it was pretty beneficial. <!-- beat:7 -->

the list of things i want to do. that i got done. and any notes / interesting things i discovered that day, etc. just dump them all in one place. <!-- beat:8 -->

maybe this is a much more natural way to organize things? <!-- beat:9 -->

like perhaps all of the data i want to capture can be derived from these daily notes. interesting artifacts, people, etc. that i discover through the day can simply go in the daily note. <!-- beat:10 -->

i actually really like this. it feels... natural. like much more natural than having to explicitly think about "taste capture", "signals" and all that stuff. <!-- beat:11 -->

just keep adding things to my notes. and that's it! <!-- beat:12 -->

let's try it out and see how it goes! <!-- beat:13 -->

any ideas that evolve into like an artifact like an essay / video or whatever can then "earn" its own doc – graduating from the daily note into its own thing. otherwise, these ideas just remain in the daily note. <!-- beat:14 -->

so basically the daily note is like a stream of consciousness associated with a time period (a day). ok, enough structure for this, let's move on. <!-- beat:15 -->

--- <!-- beat:16 -->

but damn, i really like the notion of streaming, and letting AI figure out organizing and querying and deriving from it. that is no longer my job. it's such a deep mental habit that i've reinforced over the years that is utterly useless now that i have ai. i have to be much less worried about organization and just learn to go at it and let AI do all of the chores around organizing and maintaining "me". <!-- beat:17 -->

### what i really care about <!-- beat:19 -->

i was spending some time today thinking about what i really really care about. the deepest emotion that drives everything that i do. so that perhaps i can find the direction i need to work in (like what the next step is – which is often quite unclear to me). <!-- beat:20 -->

i realize that i keep circling back to liberation, freedom, self-sovereignty. <!-- beat:21 -->

i keep coming back to the notion of discovering, cultivating, and scaling what makes us unique (that line from the π section of the [[personal-stack]]). <!-- beat:22 -->

i keep coming back to the theme of the "natural self". swadharma. and our responsibility to protect that from the incessant, constant onslaught and oppression by the world that is constantly trying to pigeonhole us, confine us, force us to conform to the mob. <!-- beat:23 -->

this isn't a new theme. today i learned about this dude john locke and this philosophy called "liberalism" that i strongly vibe with. i think this innate desire that exists in many (if not all) of us to strive for freedom and to liberate ourselves from oppression is one of the most natural desires to have as a living being. i find myself very drawn to this shape of ideas. <!-- beat:24 -->

![[IMG_0238.heic]] <!-- beat:25 -->

^ some things i wrote down during my writing sesh earlier. <!-- beat:26 -->

--- <!-- beat:27 -->

https://x.com/anuatluru/status/2120618243966861623?a=Ih5wtbj_eTk6MORJbi4&s=20 -> really really powerful observation: <!-- beat:28 -->

> You have to be willing to give up low-quality attention to get high-quality attention at the risk of getting no attention at al <!-- beat:29 -->

^ this basically points at the notion of two worlds, the growing gap b/w/ those with potential and those without, the power law eating the world. <!-- beat:30 -->

earning the attention of the "right" people is now far far more valuable than just raw attention. in fact, you're probably better off not becoming popular and only known among the ones who you want to be known by. get access to the great ideas, collaborations, deals, etc. and avoid all the BS that comes with fame. <!-- beat:31 -->

like, fame is a huge problem because it creates an impossible filtering problem. any person / institution that gets famous, now has a massive amount of noise hitting them and makes it really really difficult to identify which of the information that hits them is actually valuable. <!-- beat:32 -->

the reason why movements / communities in the early days are so powerful is because they don't have a filtering problem per se. because they require self-selection - which is the greatest filter of all because in the early days you do not gain status or other bs by joining, so you really have to join for the right reasons! <!-- beat:33 -->

why the average early YC founders were far far more competent than the average YC founder in latest batch – because they demonstrated their openness to new things, courage to stand out and not gain status, etc. just by the fact that they pursued YC when no one gave a shit about it. joining YC today is perhaps the most mainstream / conventional thing you can do as an ambitious young person. and in fact i think it's actually become a negative signal (in the same way that "MBA" was a negative signal a decade ago which basically attracted the same exact kind of person. someone striving for YC today would likely have strived for an MBA a decade and a half ago). fame/prestige = status traps. irrecoverable. <!-- beat:34 -->

great reason why there is *always* room for a new movement / community to form and displace the ones that inevitably ossify because they got too famous and thus attract all the conventionally minded folks. <!-- beat:35 -->

can you design a community / network of people that is robust to this decay due to gaining fame/prestiage/becomign a status symbol? that is one of the most valuable questions to explore. <!-- beat:36 -->

and such a problem with fame / prestige exists at the individual level too: people who get too successful lose touch with reality. anyway, more to explore about this. i think the individual fame situation can still actually be salvaged somehow. institutions that get famous are cooked tho – feels like a much much more difficult problem there. <!-- beat:37 -->

let's think about how to capture useful personal data to train π – in a way that is seamless. that (nearly) perfectly fits the way i do things, so that i don't have to "bend" myself to some specific workflow in order to collect useful data. <!-- beat:39 -->

ok, coming back to capturing data in a way that is natural. look, after that last line in the prev para, i opened up x on safari and just tweeted ("using a well crafted tool is communion with its creator"). let's just analyze what just happened to see if there is a way to capture my personal data better. <!-- beat:41 -->

so as soon as i wrote the last line in that para, i was like woah this is a great line. i decided to tweet it. i opened twitter and pasted that line in the box. i read it again and decided to make some tweaks to it to make it more sharp / powerful / better, etc. and then after making those changes i published it. <!-- beat:42 -->

but yea, let's call this workflow "streaming". basically a workflow that adapts itself to the natural "stream of consciousness" way in which all of our minds work. attention is single threaded. we start subprocesses etc. during threads, and then return to threads that started those subprocesses, etc. this is how our minds work (at least mine!). <!-- beat:62 -->

the notion of organizing / labeling etc. of things that come out of my mind is a higher order function - it does not happen as the stream is happening. like, i don't express a thought after i know which "bucket" it goes into. i figure that out after the fact. so the "organization" layer "emerges" from the stream. the core ux should not show / burden the user with organization. like if i look at this self/ dir – it is an organization of my streams of consciousness. <!-- beat:63 -->

good. ofc i can go approach what i've expressed through a neat organization layer, but i don't want to be exposed to that organization layer as i am actively expressing and exploring. <!-- beat:64 -->

- action <!-- beat:67 -->

- feed <!-- beat:68 -->

i should not have to leave my stream of consciousness for that experience! <!-- beat:85 -->

ok some thoughts: <!-- beat:168 -->

firstly i think it's a good move to set this up so that the personal data just builds organically. and that we have a clear source of truth (the stream) that can be used to generate the metadata again in the future if we ever desire (like people and posts i vibed with that i talked about, etc. – in case the way we identify them isn't that great rn). <!-- beat:169 -->

the point is, capturing the raw information is what's most valuable right now. we can figure out / re / process them again in the future to derive better insights, etc. but it's important that i start capturing it in the first place (bec. right now i'm capturing shit and it's preventing me from setting myself up well to compound from ai advancements). <!-- beat:170 -->

ok so i've been thinking a lot about superposition, and this video came up on my yt feed: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1Pit6aTfH-Y ("you weren't meant to niche yourself down") – great title that describes the superposition essence really simply. (and great video aesthetic btw). - anyway just thought it was a nice coincidence. and i def vibe with the title. and not just vibe, i think now more than ever it's important not to "niche yourself down" - because that's precisely the kind of identity that becomes fragile – ai will eat niches far faster than it will be able to touch superposition. <!-- beat:172 -->

actually on this topic of superposition: one of the reasons people overestimate ai's growth (or rather underestimate the difficulty of those doing the most difficult work in our world) is that they don't realize that the superpositional space is many orders more complex than the projections. <!-- beat:173 -->

the ceo of a great company is operating at many orders greater complexity than even their immediate reports. MANY ORDERS. people working lower in the rungs don't realize the degree of insane complexity at the top and sort of extrapolate that if ai eats their job today, then it's only a "matter of time" before it eats the top as well. which is absolutely bonkers. the rate of ai growth means that it won't hold a candle to the depths of human potential anytime in our lifetimes. that's how insanely difficult the work of the most impressive people is. (an aside: ceos are *undercompensated* by at least a couple of orders, but they will never get what they deserve because the majority of the world doesn't realize the impact / complexity of their task). <!-- beat:174 -->

^ this is a really important nuance. <!-- beat:175 -->

> π - i want you to remind me to write this essay once we have enough points for it. basically about superposition, niches, the need to rise above niches, the complexity gap between different depths in the activity tree (the notion of an activity tree), and how to think about all of this – and timely, relevant takeaways from this model to thrive in the age of superintelligence. prob check back with me on this in a week or so? i'm sure i'll express many more points on this theme in the coming days as they come to me. <!-- beat:176 -->

https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=0FhQD9pfJL4 - 9 min mark (mango tree simile) is such a profound story from ajahn chah. i respect ajahn chah immensely. one of the greatest teachers who have ever walked the earth. grateful to be able to consume his wisdom through his students, through writings, videos, and the internet. so so so grateful. this is what makes me feel blessed to be a human being – part of a lineage, a culture, of beings who (or at least sufficient number of whom) prioritized seeking truth and sharing with their fellow human beings. <!-- beat:181 -->

- this vid is by ajahn brahm (https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajahn_Brahm). <!-- beat:182 -->

- "the way to deep meditation is to let go". he gave a story of the buddha who's bowl went upstream when he place in the river symbolizing to go against the stream of craving. you cannot end craving by craving to end craving. go upstream. <!-- beat:183 -->

- "when you stay in the present moment, time disappears". all time is, is a measurement between two moments in time. in the present moment there is no way of measuring time anymore. time stands still. time disappears. and then thinking dissolves.  <!-- beat:184 -->

https://ajahnchah.org/book/index.php – fyi one of the greatest compilations of wisdom. basically his talks organized as essays. and i actually have a book of this compilation as well (which coincidentally enough i discovered at the forest book store in japantown, sf, as a free book – the publishers of this book forbade selling it, you can only give it to others for free, which is awesome btw - something i value greatly, one of the reasons i value the buddha so much - he basically forbade selling knowledge. selling knowledge is a sin. we need better "business models" for knowledge that does not involve the teacher "selling" their knowledge). <!-- beat:185 -->

i want to find people who are serious about walking this path in a non-monastic setting. ideally right here in san francisco. so that we can help each other progress on the path. as siddhartha said: friendship on the path is everything. it is what creates a virtuous culture of pushing each other in the right direction, valuing what is to be valued, and discarding what is to be discarded. <!-- beat:186 -->

"> "The reason I'm going over to the UK is [because] . . . I have a sense of responsibility to the place of my birth. It was a very wonderful society and inculcated many values in me. One of those values was fairness, where people are given equity. I came from a poor background, it was disadvantaged, but because of the fairness of the system I could, through the means of scholarships, go to a very good high school, and from [there] to a very good university" <!-- beat:188 -->

(https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajahn_Brahm) <!-- beat:189 -->

on this thought, i've been thinking recently about the importance of attention. about how now more than ever it's important to dedicate a lot of time and effort toward honing the attention. the more chaotic and noisy the world becomes, the more important and valuable it becomes to find stillness within. else you will not be able to see clearly. taking advantage of ai does not look like manic creation. ai is like a powerful sword. the more powerful it gets, the more balanced, calm, collected you must be in order to be able to wield it – so that you don't accidentally harm yourself. <!-- beat:197 -->

ok i think i have a good picture of what i want, let's get into it: <!-- beat:206 -->

1. what data do i want to capture? <!-- beat:207 -->

	- the stream (basically all of my notes) <!-- beat:208 -->

	- artifacts (creation, iteration, publishing of things that i make like essays, videos, products, etc.). <!-- beat:209 -->

	- the way my attention moves across everything (across stream, docs, chat, browsing, how i engage with others, etc.) – essentially the way i "wield" my attention. <!-- beat:210 -->

	- how i consume and engage with information: <!-- beat:211 -->

		- what sites i go to, what i do on those sites. <!-- beat:212 -->

		- how i scroll feeds and engage on social (which posts do i stop on, like, bookmark, reply to, follow, etc.) <!-- beat:213 -->

	- actions: <!-- beat:214 -->

		- reaching out to people <!-- beat:215 -->

		- how i build  <!-- beat:216 -->

	- inbox (see below how i expect an agent to filter notifs that reach me by appending to my inbox – which i process. so i want to know which notifs i act on, which ones i ignore, etc. etc.) <!-- beat:217 -->

	- $ transactions: this is a v powerful (and honest) signal of my priorities. i want to set up a mercury card for self/ (and also let my agents access it to purchase things) and track each and every financial cost - including tokens, services i use, storage, etc. – basically i want to capture my finances with regards to me expressing myself in this way - my day to day stuff! any private transactions can go on my boa card, and all the transactions i want to track can just go through my mercury. simple. <!-- beat:218 -->

		- it would be good to set this up sooner rather than later. <!-- beat:219 -->

2. what services do i need? <!-- beat:220 -->

	- i will need some notion of agents: { model, instructions, examples, history }[] <!-- beat:221 -->

	- i will need services (webhooks, apis – 3rd party but also our own). <!-- beat:222 -->

	- i will need some kind of orchestration(?) – cron jobs, event based (when x happens, launch y agent with the following prompt). <!-- beat:223 -->

	- i will need a thorough log of everything that happens here so that we can improve them. <!-- beat:224 -->

	- (eventually) i will need some notion of our own models that represent my taste that my agents can use (such as a model of what i vibe with that my agent can use when scouring web to find people and expressions i'd vibe with!). <!-- beat:225 -->

notes: <!-- beat:226 -->

- i want to keep things as "native" as possible. no new ux, nothing besides just this "filesystem" ux. perhaps a terminal to see the live cron jobs / services, etc.? the point is that everything is represented minimally - in just text and basic, fundamental, universal, open file formats, etc. <!-- beat:227 -->

	- for ex: i view inbox/ as just a directory. the README is the main inbox, and each file within the dir is each "post" that my agent thinks i should give my attention to. in the README a simple one line description of it is given, and when i click the link it opens up the actual post for that notif that contains more info (including timestamp metadata etc.). to archive it, i simply delete its line from the README! the post is still there so we basically continue to know what i saw even after i "archive" it. you know? this may not be a flow that works idk, but you get my point that this should be native in some way. and this means we should be able to track clicks within the md docs - like when i click and it opens up that other doc, this should be info that we have! that way we know which inbox items i completely ignore, which ones i actually open, etc. etc. – all super useful information for the attention_agent we will create that will do its best to only surface things worth my time! <!-- beat:228 -->

a really powerful short film esque scene that i want to make one day. it's an opening shot to the film / video or whatever. i'm not even sure what the entire artifact is going to be, but i can see the opening crystal clearly: <!-- beat:230 -->

i'm in a white room. white on all sides. sterile. a hospital setting. <!-- beat:231 -->

the shot opens with me in the center of the frame. there is a wooden chair right behind me, so the audience can't see it yet, but i'm standing right in front of it. <!-- beat:232 -->

i look around to the left and right with a look of panic. 5 white men in suits (agent smith esque) walk briskly at me from all sides, push me back onto the chair and pin me to the chair as i fight back intensely trying to break free. <!-- beat:233 -->

but i can't. <!-- beat:234 -->

camera: as they are pushing me back toward the chair, the camera is tracking. after i've sat in the chair, the camera zooms into my eyes. linear zoom, not on a curve, just slowly getting to my face, and once my face is centered, it starts slantedly upward toward my eyes - so that my eyes are centered. i am furious and afraid. <!-- beat:235 -->

one of the agents stabs my third eye region with some kind of syringe. i stop resisting after that lost in some kind of trance - as if my soul is leaving my body. the camera keeps zooming in and stops once it's literally my eyes in the frame. like so zoomed in that the entire frame is basically my eyes. after the syringe injected me, my eyes turned white. <!-- beat:236 -->

after stopping briefly at my white eyes for a moment (during which the music stops and everything goes silent), the camera starts zooming out. and as it is zooming out it shows me in the same suit the agents are wearing, sitting in an open office floor plan with many other folks with suits working behind me, as i sit there still looking straight at the audience with my white eyes, a dead expression, as i am typing away on my laptop on the desk right in front of me. <!-- beat:237 -->

the camera basically reverses the same movement symmetrically. zoom out and then track backward to show the office. <!-- beat:238 -->

to me this scene represents the way in which the world kills those who are too different. the world forces conformity and beheads the bold voices. <!-- beat:239 -->

if i can make this scene the way i see it in my mind's eye, i will have fucking goosebumps. such a powerful scene that represents one of my deepest fears: the fear of standing out too much, the fear of leaning into my uniqueness too much because i fear that society will fuck me over for doing so. this scene basically represents my own fear of standing out. that's the thing, this isn't actually a commentary on the world, or a criticism of the world, it is a criticism of myself – of my own fear, of my own mind that projects its fears onto the world. but the world is also to be blamed to some degree – because otherwise such a fear (which i believe many share if not consciously, at least unconsciously – else why the fuck would all of us be so conformative and conventional?) would not have become such a predominant evolutionary conditioning. <!-- beat:240 -->

anyway. there's something just so fucking powerful about this scene. idk. can't even put it in words. i want to make this. it's not even 20s / 30s long. just a simple "short film" in and of itself. in fact, this scene IS the artifact. there is no need for anything else. <!-- beat:241 -->

π – bring this up at some point in the next few weeks, as a reminder, and if i feel like it then i can start shaping this up more seriously and actually shoot it. <!-- beat:242 -->

and even before i start visualizing it, let me walk through some of the thoughts i had earlier today while thinking about this: <!-- beat:255 -->

1) set up the workspace and get the basic use cases down (including setting up agents on cron, reacting to events, etc.) and tracking all of the data that we want. nothing else matters until we can start making things happen from within the workspace itself. and then through the workspace we can iterate on the workspace. <!-- beat:256 -->

2) no need to worry about privacy (what is public / private, etc.), or even how to persist things. all of that can come later after we have the core use cases tackled. <!-- beat:257 -->

3) the core use cases are: <!-- beat:258 -->

	1) stream <!-- beat:259 -->

	2) docs <!-- beat:260 -->

	3) chat <!-- beat:261 -->

	4) browser <!-- beat:262 -->

	6) control center – live processes, cron jobs, etc. <!-- beat:264 -->

	7) logging – everything. every single thing i do in this workspace, everything that π does within this workspace, is logged. <!-- beat:265 -->

4) after we get the core use cases down, then everything else can be built on this organically. <!-- beat:266 -->

5) source of truth is just markdown files. wherever possible we just represent things in markdown. we may create views "on top" of this markdown for convenience sake (that say we can view on the website), but the workspace itself relies on markdown as much as possible. i don't even want spreadsheets or anything. just simple markdown files for lit. everything. like, i don't want us to build much product. just v basic high level unification of the various panes we need to do everything in one workspace, and that's about it. hopefully. <!-- beat:267 -->

ok. some things to think through here. i want to picture some of the important use cases. or rather, the following are just random ideas - not yet prioritized. super rough. most won't make it, but i feel like getting them out there: <!-- beat:268 -->

- there is a settings/ dir that has a tooltip.md file that dictates what tooltips i see when i highlight anything in the workspace. for ex: one that i would really appreciate: highlight text in the stream and see tooltips for: "post on x", "create note", "open in chat", "search on web", that basically let me quickly act on that region i highlighted. and the source of truth for instructions for what to do when it is selected is represented in tooltip.md in **natural language**. and to map that natural language to the exact commands to execute in the workspace we can use a vvv fast local llm that just runs on device so that it feels instantaneous. OR, if that doesn't work, then the tooltip.md file can just have the actual commands to run to execute it - which can be managed through the chat (like i tell chat what i want it to do, and it goes and writes the code for that tooltip action in a codeblock in tooltip.md – which will have a schema we adhere to). <!-- beat:269 -->

	- this is a powerful way to set things up. and it lends itself vv well to π just editing and managing things on my behalf, while i can go check it out super easily. <!-- beat:270 -->

- and similar to the way tooltips are set up: we do the same thing for the cmdk menu - just a schema that we have that determines what we see in the cmdk menu depending on the context (like when i press it while editing doc, it shows one thing, if i launch it while browsing, it shows a diff set of options - and this is fully customizable). – and this too would just be an md file in settings/. in fact, let's simplify: no tooltip at all, only cmd k! so if i highlight text and press cmd k - it shows me the specific options for when a text is highlighted. this is super flexible. and we can make it vv context dependent. ofc if i type then it basically searches through all the possible actions in the workspace i can take. this will be a v important surface area that will make the workspace a blast to use. <!-- beat:271 -->

- as i was writing the above stuff, it's now clear that we need a sort of "api" for the workspace app. like, editing files and stuff not really - π can just use the system operations to edit files and whatnot. but i also want π to be able to "control" the workspace itself: change which files are open, open the browser, open things in the browser, do things in the webpages, launch agents, etc. so i'm not sure what this api needs to have, but i'm guessing there is some kind of api here that π can use to have full control over the workspace however it wants. <!-- beat:272 -->

- logging needs to be comprehensive. and i think it is a top level organization alongside <3, π, and $ – because the logs cover EVERYTHING in the entire workspace. logs/ – would have like browsing/, transactions/, actions/, etc. – i mean we can work together to figure out the organization here, maybe it's actually simpler and better to just make this one massive blob thing splunk etc. that just keeps getting appended to and we can just process that with π independently to extract / organize the right context in the right way to be useful in chats? im not sure, something to think about. <!-- beat:273 -->

 - coming back to the api: i want π to have control over literally everything. like, for example, i'm not ever going to re-arrange the order of the dirs. i will just command π through the chat and it will arrange them in the order i want. within some dirs i may want it ordered by lastEditedTimestamp, in others i may want it in another way. the point is i will never "drag and drop" or other bs at all. commanding π will be my primary means of "doing things" in the workspace - including editing the workspace state itself. there are some exceptions ofc, like i may just have a keyboard / cmd center shortcut to open the browser / chat or something. like that stuff would be nice, but by and large we should NOT build ux to do things – assume everything just modified through chat, including settings, etc. <!-- beat:289 -->

(I DISAGREE WITH THIS, IT SHOULD BE RIGHT HERE, because why the fuck not? lol. we can discuss this). <!-- beat:290 -->

 - for the app organization: <!-- beat:294 -->

	 - i see the filesystem on the left <!-- beat:295 -->

(on this note, the command center - cmdk - menu will be represented in json obv now that we have established the representation principle - i will never edit this directly!). <!-- beat:306 -->

because think about it: my personal data is the ground truth of the shape of my being. my pitch is a fiction. a fiction about what i think i will be able to do and whatnot. but inherently the pitch is a story. ofc i'll still have a pitch maybe to talk about how i plan to keep evolving – oh, that's actually really important, my pitch is how i am intentionally, actively shaping the shape of my thinking, whereas the personal data will be the shape of my thinking until now. and the truth is that BOTH can be true! <!-- beat:314 -->

why? because i have free will. i can condition my conditioning. this goes back to something siddhartha talked about – your past karma (conditioning) dictates what sankharas emerge in the present, but how you react (ideally don't react) to the sankharas determines how you condition your conditioning right now – that shapes the nature of the sankharas that will emerge in the future. <!-- beat:315 -->

ok that was a roundabout way of saying that we have free will. we can condition our conditioning. and thus my pitch will be about how i intend to evolve myself and what i plan to do in order to do that. my personal data will be what i have done till now – basically my past conditioning! <!-- beat:316 -->

and naturally one would need to see both to have all the information they'd need to invest in me. <!-- beat:317 -->

--- <!-- beat:321 -->

ok. lets get into this. first time i think im streaming on my phone. damn a keyboard might be necessary for this im not sure. <!-- beat:322 -->

on streaming: <!-- beat:323 -->

when i say streaming im not talking about live streaming but rather a way of operating, perhaps you could even call it a "workflow".  <!-- beat:324 -->

when i say streaming, im referring to streaming your consciousness. thoughts arise in the mind and you translate those thoughts into text with no gap between the thought and the expression of that thought into text. <!-- beat:325 -->

ok but practically speaking, there will be a gap. but ive found by practice that you can shave that gap down to be really small, and eventually dissolve it altogether. when the gap is dissolved, you become a witness to the thoughts emerging in your mind and flowing out of you onto the screen in front of you – as one continuous flow, without you impeding it in any way. <!-- beat:326 -->

of course there is certainly some subconscious pruning that is going on. your subconscious is probably figuring out what trains of thought to allow and which to hide - because it feels vulnerable to see your thinking in words somehow, and your mind probably prevents you from seeing some of your deepest hidden trains of thought that it may not want to admit? im not sure how or what happens under the hood, but im sure theres something going on that is pruning / determining what trains to allow to be streamed. <!-- beat:327 -->

ok but thats kind of besides the point. what is the point im trying to get at? ok what is streaming and why is it useful? thats the crux of what im trying to explore rn. <!-- beat:328 -->

ok so streaming is simple: its basically a stream of consciousness: expressing your thoughts as they arise in the mind. if it is a visual, you can draw it on a piece of paper and drop the pic of it in the stream. you can describe it in words, whatever. the point is to get out of the way of your mind and allow it to move naturally. thats the point. there is no forcing. "you" do not determine what you think about. you do not force a direction that your mind is not feeling like exploring in the moment. you literally allow whatever emerges to express itself. <!-- beat:329 -->

the more you do it, the more easily you can dissociate between you and your thoughts. it becomes very clear that your thoughts emerge on their own due to your conditioning, and that you dont really actively "think". shows just how clunky our language is to desribe the process of thinking. it isnt an active process that you sort of put effort and do. it happens. <!-- beat:330 -->

so does that mean we have no control? ofc not. we certainly do. and our control is restricted to our attention. you can choose to let go of a thought and not "build" on it to form a train of thoughts, and wait for the next one. <!-- beat:331 -->

you have the choice for which thoughts you pay attention to and build on, and which ones you let go of. <!-- beat:332 -->

ok that language was too strong. i think its more accurate to say that you can have such control over attention, but it is a skill to be worked on, and does not come naturally to most (if any) of us. <!-- beat:333 -->

what you pay attention to, gains energy. if you have a negative thought, and fail to let go of it and hold onto it with your attention and allow it to form a train - you can quickly enter into a negative spiral. and ofc the same with positive. actually maybe not, it seems positive cycles are more difficult? ok i have to clarify what positive means here. will deal with this nuance later. basically my point is that peace is not found in these reactive trains of thought trains / spirals, peace is found in letting them go, so its imp to distinguish between negative, positive and peace. like positive trains can lead to mania, and negative trains can lead to anxiety, and peace is a diff dimension altogether. <!-- beat:334 -->

ok where was i? alright im talking about streaming. streaming is the process of translating your thinking into an external form (text drawings etc) as they emerge with little to no "gap" in which you process it before you express it. that is what i mean by streaming. <!-- beat:335 -->

why? why do i value streaming? what is so valuable about this process? why now? there is something about this new state of our world that enables and rewards streaming in ways that didnt make sense before. lets explore all of this. <!-- beat:336 -->

and maybe before that let me talk about some of the "best practices" ive found. well they prob arent anywhere close to best, but whatever, they feel like improvements to how i used to this before. <!-- beat:337 -->

firstly, a stream goes in one direction. there is no going back and tweaking something youve expressed. now, you can def go back and redact something youve said, bec maybe its private and you dont want it in an external form. that may be valuable, or at least knowing you can do that lets you ease up a bit, but ok besides redacting you cant go back and start shaping something within the stream in the stream itself. <!-- beat:338 -->

for ex: you cant go up to say a part of the stream that you think would be something good to post on twitter or turn into an essay and start editing it right there in the stream. that is not allowed. if you want to "shape" an expression into an artifact that you publish or whatever, you copy it from the stream and paste it elsewhere and craft it. <!-- beat:339 -->

the stream is meant to be a one way flow forward. like time. you cant go backward. <!-- beat:340 -->

why is streaming useful? <!-- beat:341 -->

firstly, idk now any of this will apply to others. this is a very personal account of this process  and i have no idea how any of this generalizes. <!-- beat:342 -->

but for me, ive noticed that i censor myself a lot. i can get trapped in this dumb sort of editing loop where i write a paragraph and i keep tweaking it without moving forward. and what happens is that i lose my natural train of thought when i do that. which leads me to lose my natural shape of expression. <!-- beat:343 -->

even when i stream, the first few minutes are slow because im still impeding the process. it takes some time to let go, but once i do it starts flowing freely. <!-- beat:344 -->

and the more free it flows the more natural it is. the natural shape of my being starts to manifest without me impeding it. <!-- beat:345 -->

and this is powerful. i feel like my best ideas my best work emerge when i allow my mind to move naturally without imposing myself onto it. and this notion of natural self, swadharma, wuwei etc. is not new. great minds throughout history have talked about the notion of an unpolluted natural self that we find so difficult to support, and we often suppress it. <!-- beat:346 -->

anyway, ive found that streaming allows my natural trains of thought to emerge. it stills my sense of self and reduces me imposing myself onto my natural self. if that makes sense. <!-- beat:347 -->

streaming is something ive only felt comfortable doing recently. why? bec im a lazy ass. pre ai i wouldnt have ever considered doing this bec i would have been put off by the laborious work of going thru the stream finding interesting things, developing them etc. its all just so much fucking work. ai changes that completely. <!-- beat:348 -->

streaming really benefits from this sort of "background" or "ambient" intelligence: a system that can make sense of the stream, produce artifacts from it, etc. <!-- beat:349 -->

so coming back to the process. i think well i have a hunch that streaming is the core "work" that i will do. as ai gets better, translating the natural movements of my mind into things that create value for myself / others will be what ai will do for me. this natural shape of my expression is the most important, personal unique contribution i predict i will have (if i have any contribution that is lol). <!-- beat:350 -->

ok lets look at that idea again. i see streaming as the main sort of work that i do. ai will increasingly be responsible for turning stream into artifacts we publish to world, and will be responsible for pulling my attention toward things that are happening outside that it thinks i should know about - which then naturally impacts my stream. <!-- beat:351 -->

so again: a stream of consciousness, and inputs and outputs to my mind. thats literally all i am as a person i guess. inputs is any information. like cool ideas people have posted. someone reaching out to me. and outputs are things that emerge from my stream and are shipped to the world as artifacts: ex essays products videos etc. <!-- beat:352 -->

thats it. streaming + ai is what i think will be my primary way of working. <!-- beat:353 -->

in a way ai will be like a semi permeable layer that protects my attention from things that arent worth my time, and shows me what i want to actually consume, and translates anything valuable from my stream to reality. <!-- beat:354 -->

naturally i will have the final say on what i actually ship to the world. thats the other thing, most of my streaming will be useless! power laws are natural. less than 0.001% of my stream will lead to anything valuable. the rest will likely be garbage. thats ok! <!-- beat:355 -->

anyway, this is how i see humans working with ai. and i am very bullish on humans, not because i think that we are superior in any way, but because i believe that nature produces an intelligence that we can never match through the things we produce. <!-- beat:356 -->

thats the thing. i believe ai will never surpass human potential because i believe the max of human potential is the potential of nature itself. and i think it is impossible for humans to create a form of intelligence that is better than what nature can. <!-- beat:357 -->

why do i believe max of human potential is equal to nature potential? prob some combo of intuition and brainwashing? lol. look, i grew up in a home that exposed me to great minds like siddhartha gautama. and one of the key points ive been exposed to is that when we dig deep within ourselves we will find all of nature within us. or rather when we dissolve the notion of the self we become one with nature and can therefore experience all of nature. i believe this to be true. and so if this is true, the limit of human capacity is the limit of nature itself. <!-- beat:358 -->

now ofc i am vvvv far away from this limit. and i think most of us are. prob all living humans rn are some ways away from this limit. and so many of us may be made irrelevant by ai. i may be useless compared to ai. but i will never accept that situation to always be true. i will always believe that if i access my potential to a greater depth, i will be able to create value for humanity that ai cannot. and i believe this is true for each and every human being. which is why my bio / tagline is "i believe in human potential". this is an unshakeable belief i have, and i cant imagine anything that would change this. bec even if we enter a world in which ai is better than all of us at everything, i will still believe that such a state will not last as we dig to realize our human potential. basically my belief is structured in a way that there will never be any evidence that would change it. like if i become useless and never unlock my potential to a sufficient degree and die useless, i will basically take responsibility and believe that it was simply because i could not realize my potential and realize the natural intelligence nature has gifted each and every one of us (if only we can access it). <!-- beat:359 -->

ok coming back to streaming. or rather from here on i will be operating on the belief that ai will never surpass human potential for reasons mentioned above. given that to be true in my model of the world, i see streaming + ai processing stream and guarding attention to be the ultimate workflow that allows me to lean into my natural intelligence as ai does all the bs to translate natural intelligence into useful creations. <!-- beat:360 -->

thats the thing. streaming is useful bec it is a way to allow the natural movement of my mind to emerge without me impeding it. <!-- beat:361 -->

look at this stream so far. tangents galore. there is no linear trains. thats how my natural mind works! not sure how this is for others, but this is clearly a pattern ive observed with my own thinking. <!-- beat:362 -->

one thought leads to another forming a train that then branches off into another train and it keeps going. fractals, trees, basically the shape of nature itself: rivers, trees, etc all follow this shape of expression. i suspect this would be the natural shape for every person. <!-- beat:363 -->

now what thought we have and how we branch off and what we choose to give attention to and let go - that will be incredibly unique to each of us. but the shape of consciousness i think is the same for all of us and everything in nature. <!-- beat:364 -->

ok so i consider streaming to be the core thing that i do. the core piece of my workflow. and ai is what does everything else for me: shows me what is worth seeing, and translates my stream into things i put out into the world. <!-- beat:365 -->

 and so a question ive been exploring these past few days is: what is the optimal "workspace" / tool given my belief in streaming? and also, importantly, how to train ai to perform its role really well, allowing me to spend more time on just streaming and doing the things i want to do - walking thru nature, connecting with people etc.! <!-- beat:366 -->

like i want to lean into living a life that is aligned with my nature, while having ai translate my natural self into things that help me create (and capture some of) value for the world. <!-- beat:367 -->

thats my core research theme if i could call it that. <!-- beat:368 -->

alright im a bit tired pecking away at my phone. sitting here at this new cafe that opened up in the castro well its a new branch they have anotherr somewhere else in the city but this place is dope. and popping. jandii i think is the name. anyway my hands hurt. i usually stream with a keyboard but yea phone felt convenient given im out and about but this hurts damn. <!-- beat:369 -->

alos for me dictation doesnt let me stream. when i say something i censor and impede far more. writing silently gives me the feeling that this is all still "internal" and not something ive put out into the world. <!-- beat:370 -->

i think this is a good thing. i want my speaking to be much more intentional. i want to be very thoughtful about what i put out into the world. blabbering whatever comes to mind or shipping the stream as is without taking care into shaping the right artifacts breaks noble speech. noble speech i think is something i aspire to. i think its a beautiful idea. and an important priority and practice for me. so yea i dont want to train blabbering speech which is why i dont stream through dictation. nor would i ever stream through video. maybe something neuralink like down the line (that would be ideal tbh), but until then something in text form. <!-- beat:371 -->

coming back to the workspace: <!-- beat:372 -->

stream as the core surface for my expression. an inbox where ai surfaces things that deserve my attention. and everything else hidden from me i guess. ok theres more nuance to this. i def want to see and iterate on an artifact before shipping. ai is nowhere near being good enough to craft great artifacts to ship. but i guess the better ai gets and rhe better it understsnds my taste, the more i can just stream. that feels right. <!-- beat:373 -->

what does ai need to learn about me to help surface the right things for me to put my attention on, protect my attention from bs, and translate my stream into artifacts to ship? this is a critical q to explore. <!-- beat:374 -->

before we move on just want to talk about something cool. was walking through the panhandle and stopped by the basketball court to just shoot around with a couple of folks there. ended up having a nice casual convo with them. cool peeps. one of them - liza - was exceptional at asking questions that made me really pause and appreciate that skill. one of the greatest skills. most conversations are thoughtless, unintentional blabbering of sorts. you go into them without really needing to be mindful or expecting to really have to think. oh nice weather something sports yay. i mean dont get me wrong small talk and normal convos are great, it breaks the ice and enables deeper interaction, but some people have the knack of asking really thoughtful questions that pull you out of the mindless monotonous convos and makes you really pay attention and actually articulate / process something. they were incredible at asking such questions. and in a way that was warm, welcoming, and not weird at all - like even without smalltalk as a warm up. great skill. and i came away thinking id like to cultivate that skill. it requires being really aware of the person and the situation and being just good about creating that subtle tension of a great question. thats the other thing, a great question creates the right tension: you can no longer be lax and just coast thru the conversation or situation mindlessly. anyway, something to note for myself. <!-- beat:375 -->

back to the ai stuff. there are two parts to this question. firstly what do i need to "show" it so that it understsnds my taste, and secondly, how does it actually learn it. <!-- beat:376 -->

both are distinct. i can capture great examples of, and write a book about my taste, but if i show it to someone unskilled, or say a dog, they arent going to be able to draw the right intuitions and insights from that information to be able to model me. <!-- beat:377 -->

but for now im not going to worry about the latter. as ai gets more powerful, i think it will just get really good at drawing the right model given great examples and instructions and whatnot. <!-- beat:378 -->

ie, given the right data, ai should get great at modeling things. whether that process is called "fine tuning" or something else or just abstracted away entirely by the ai doesnt matter to us. <!-- beat:379 -->

so i think the more pertinent question is: what is the right personal data i need to capture so that ai can perform its role above in my workflow really well? <!-- beat:380 -->

i think this comes down to capturing all meaningful expressions of my uniqueness. ofc that involves taste, intuition, judgement and whatnot, there are many overused terms here, but it really comes down to capturing the shape of "me" whatever that means. <!-- beat:381 -->

i guess even more fundamentally it is about the shape in which my mind "moves". its pattern. what triggers it to do something. what does it observe. how does it sift through information to find something valuable? what questions arise in it? etc. etc.  <!-- beat:382 -->

i mean there are so so many signals of this. where attention stops when i read an essay, watch a video, how the mind moves from one idea to another, the ways in which the mind iterates on an artifact, when it feels its good enough to ship, etc. <!-- beat:383 -->

what it observes, understands, how it values, predicts, moves. etc. <!-- beat:384 -->

many of this i dont think i even have words for. <!-- beat:385 -->

more importantly, i am not capturing even a fraction of this useful data. which means how can ai help me when i lit dont have what it needs to see in order to have a shot at making sense of me. <!-- beat:386 -->

see going back to the examples above, how will ai know which information to surface when if it doesnt understand what i vibe with, and when i vibe with it and when i want to vibe with it etc. even a personal assistant who follows a person all day every day for a decade can only get so good at this. <!-- beat:387 -->

see doing chores is easy. this is what most "persknal agents" do today. its easy to know how to book flight tickets or whatnot for me. but it is incredibly sifficult to construct the right feed of information for me to consume, or translate a high level prompt for, say the kind of home i want, into actually executing all of the minute decisions in the way i would - in a way thats aligned with my taste. <!-- beat:388 -->

my vision for personal ai is to scale my uniqueness. i should essentially be able to "call myself" to go do things in the way i would. i would like to scale myself to go sit and incessantly scroll all thefeeds in the world and surface the right things to me so i dont have to corrupt my attention wading thru all the bs to find the gems worth my attention. <!-- beat:389 -->

that is what i want. and to do that well, ai needs to really understand me - my taste jusgsment intuition shape of my unique mind patterns etc. <!-- beat:390 -->

and on this note i think each of us is incredibly unique. i believe there will come a day where the data each of us captures of our uniqueness will be greater than all of the data we use to train general models today, and that our "personal models" will be larger and more complex than today's frontier general models. <!-- beat:391 -->

which leads to the next point: each of us will - or rather anyone serious about their craft - will need to capture, curate our personal data and train ai about what we are, so that it can actually help us realize our potential in the ways that we want to, in a way that is aligned with what we are, essentially scaling us. <!-- beat:392 -->

ok so where am i? streaming, workspace, ai, personal data. <!-- beat:393 -->

great. now how to capture my personal data? what data to capture? how do draw signal from it? how to determine when an ai action is not good enough and when it is good enough and use that to reinforce / iterate on ais understanding of me. <!-- beat:394 -->

ok this is good, this is uncovering sub components / some nuance to this. <!-- beat:395 -->

data of me is used by ai to do things for me. how good or bad it does those things becomes further context for it to use the next time it does those things. essentially my feedback to ai also becomes part of my personal data - and ofc that makes sense bec my feedback too is an expression of my taste / judgement etc. <!-- beat:396 -->

and whats imp is that all of this data is captured naturally. i should not have to do anything different from how i naturally instinctively do things bec if i had to then i just wouldnt. remember im a lazy ass. <!-- beat:397 -->

even the feedback. i dont want to explicitly give it feedback. like if it surfaces something and i just ignore it - that becomes something it learns from. maybe sometimes i give it feedback actively but only if it comes naturally. like if im iterating on an artifact with ai then ofc i would explicitly have to say not like this or do this or whatever. but when active feebdack is not part of my natural flow, i dont want to / or rather i wont give it explicit feedback. it just needs to be able to see everything i do in order to understand whether it created value with that action or not. <!-- beat:398 -->

like if ai showed me a person it thought i should connnect with but i dont end up dming or emailing them them ai should know that its recommendation wasnt acted on. now whether it determines timing was not right or whether they are not a good fit or whatever is something it will figure out. maybe in this example i can say explicitly why, but the truth is, i myself may not know! the vibe simply may not feel right and i wont act on it. and no one including me or ai will know perfectly why. and thats ok! forcing me to explain introduces lies into the data bec i genuinely think a lot of my reasoning about why i like or dont like something is likely bs. lol. i dont trust it. and so why should ai? <!-- beat:399 -->

like our reasoning and explanations for our feelings are clouded / polluted by our subconscious fears and cravings and whatnot. remember when you had to break up with someone and you told yourself it was bec of xyz reason only later to realize not really it was actually bec of abc reason only to later realize that wssnt it either and you dont really know why. like your subconscious makes so many decisions that you simply cannot accurately explain. this is a feature of the disconnect between feelings and reasoning that i think exists in most of us. <!-- beat:400 -->

and the more valuable data, where my uniqueness lies, is in feeling - in vibes - not in reasoning. <!-- beat:401 -->

and this is what the wise mentioned as well. the most profound truths are beyond the reach of words. reasoning can be captured by words, which means that reasoning can never explain or represent the most valuable truths. <!-- beat:402 -->

thats all i mean by artifact: something that is ready to be shipped to world. and that is a high bar. bec of noble speech. it must be truthful. timely, useful, beneficial. todo: i want to write about noble speech at some point. i think it needs an expansion to also include new mediums. like for ex: streaming doesnt need noble speech - it is an honest expression of whatever is in mind, but posting certainly does. so i need a clear framework for noble speech for my own workflow and tools and mediums and the notion of an internet and whatnot. and this leads to thinking about what is public and what remains private. there is an entire privacy architecture here that is important to think through. extreme transparency or privacy are both flawed. there is a middle path here that is optimal. prob biased toward transparency. and the middle optimal path is always changing as well. the better our tech gets at preserving and guaranteeing privacy, the better ai becomes etc. how transparwnt i will be will need to adapt to stay optimal. everytjing is connected. no such thing as a rigid fixed "middle path" for anything. ok maybe just most things. not sure. <!-- beat:405 -->

the point ehre is that, well there are a few points here. where do i start? rn i'm streaming iwth my eyes closed. ok also "streaming", like giving htis process a name and shit makes it sound like its' some fancy special thing but it's really not. was walking with a friend today and she was like or rather we were talking about wlaks in general and what goes in in the mind when you walk. and she brought up that she just has an internal monologue of sorts that's always going on and she like discusses / argues with a version / part of her self which is what sort of creates that monologue. that happens wiht me too! i think that happens with most of us! this is why meditation is like a process that goes "against the grain" as ajahn chah likes to refer to it – it is the process of letting the thoguhts flow without adding to / latching onto / messign with them / amplifying them / reacting to them with more thoughts. and as you do that, eventually the thoughts subside and you start to be able to perceive the underlying truths about our experience that are normally overshadowed by our thinking. <!-- beat:418 -->

so my point is that "streaming" is really something i think all of us do most of the time. this is the interneal monologue going on incessantly as the mind keeps thinking and thinking about all sorts of things. and this "streaming" is just externalizing that thinking onto the screen / into a form that ai can see. <!-- beat:419 -->

that's what streaming is about. streaming is basically like neuralink before having neuralink. allowing ai to access the internal thinking going on within our minds, so that it can start to act on things for us as they emerge in our minds. that's all this is. so it's nothing special. it's a really simple idea really. <!-- beat:420 -->

which is why let's say im streaming and a question comes to my mind that may start a new thread, i may shoot it off to π and just keep moving on the current thread without worrying about the side thread knowing that i will get back to it at some later point - after π has alreday laid the groundwork, done the research or whatever for that question and we can progress with it. <!-- beat:437 -->

ok so yea think of this like a river system. that's how the mind flows. it flows like a river. sometimes it breaks off into multiple sub rivers that may futher branch off. now, consciousness is single threaded, which means that i myself can only move across these rivers (tangents / branches, etc.) in a one-thing-at-a-time way. but ai changes that. i can continue on one flow while π is actually handling many different sub-flows that we have uncovered as part of the main flow while i just keep going. and the better ai gets, the more i can just stay on the most natural flow wihtout worrying about / deciding when to go into a subflow to sharpne / shape the idea and ship and when to come back tot he main flow. <!-- beat:438 -->

my thesis about ai is that ai will do all of the work that has no room for individual uniqueness. pushing each of us toward doing what only we can do – pushing us to lean into our uniqueness. and so if we view this stream from that lens, the better ai gets at executing my ideas, the more time and attention i spend purely streaming. that's how my thesis reflects practically in the way i work. <!-- beat:445 -->

ok then if this is what i see let me start to design the ux for this workflow that i think would best suit what i'm feeling about streaming and how i intend to work moving forward. <!-- beat:446 -->

the core piece is the streaming surface. it is basically a text file. a really really really long text file. streaming has no formatting. no dividers. no headers. nothing. it is just information that represents the movement of my mind – as natural as possible. that's it.  <!-- beat:447 -->

all organization, all interpretations, all whatever that stem from this stream is something that π is responsible for setting up. that being said, i think at least now, because i don't think ai is good enough yet, i think for now i will also be in charge of dictating when to "launch" π to start a sub-thread. <!-- beat:448 -->

ok, this is a really cool model of this. consciousness is single-threaded. but ai allows you to make your consciousness multi-threaded. the better π represents "me", the more i can act in the world in a multi-threaded way. π will do things in the way i would if i did them, and i can just focus on the higher level flow that is natural to my mind - that there is no way in hell that π could produce on its own (because it's too unique!). <!-- beat:450 -->

people think ai is going to replace all humans for everything and i think that's dumb. let's just say for example that ai COULD replicate this stream. would it have the time to? time is still scarce right? you could put all the compute in our solar system together and get ai to just produce streams of consciousnesses to replicate / produce the valuable streams that humans could, it would still take time to cover the space of all possibilities. because intelligence is single threaded. the more threads you want of ai, the more compute and stuff you need. and it's just all really difficult to do. impossible. anyway, that's besides the point. i have to remind myself that i don't need to "prove" all of my beliefs. i already know to the core of my being that ai is not going to replace all humans for everthing we do. it's really that simple. because i know that fully, that there is no way to convince me otherwise, i should waste absolutely no time trying to "prove" that either to myself or to anyone else. <!-- beat:451 -->

sometiems i think we try to prove things out of insecurity. or rather the desire to be understood by others, which prob stems from the desire to belong, to fit in. a deep evolutionary conditioning because back in the day if you didn't fit in you'd prob die. getting ostracized by your tribe meant death for most of human history. so our biology, our  nervous system, is overindexed on being accepted by others because that urge was probably the most imp conditioning to keep us alive – to survive and reproduce. so it's basically nearly-completely outdated hardware. ok nearly-completely is a stretch, but definitely over-indexed by at least a few orders. the more human rights are established and enforced in our world – the more human freedom and individual expression are protected, the more outdated our urge to fit in becomes. we are still a ways away from getting to where we need to be. there are many artists today who, in many parts of the world, are marked as insane and ostracized, tortured, or worse. it's a dark world in many parts. these freedoms we enjoy in the free world are rare. they are not the norm. and they haven't been the norm for most of human history. and it's our responsibility to protect them and build on them. this isn't an easy task, but for anyone who thinks different, for anyone creative, you owe to the countless others who staked their lives for you and i to have this freedom, to preserve and cultivate it for the many who will come after us. this is one of the deepest responsibilities that i feel. <!-- beat:452 -->

ok so coming back to the streaming ux. another thing here is i'm fascinated by the way my mind explores an idea. when i think of ux for a product i'm working on, my mind naturally goes all over the place. it's a nonlinear approach. and i have to trust the process. like thinking about the importance of protecting individual freedoms - what the hell does that have anything to do with designing this product? oh but it has everything to do with it. i just can't see it yet. i may never be able to understand, but it is connected. it's just nonlinear. you can't reason about it. and so for most of my life i've suppressed this kind of meandering. i've suppressed the natural movement of my mind to force it to stay on the "task at hand" as if that was somehow superior to surrendering to it and letting it show me what it wants to show me. <!-- beat:453 -->

also like i said the music i listen to has a deep impact on what i think and how i think. which means that a critical data to collect is all the sounds that enter into me as i stream / work. which means that this workspace should collect that information. right now i have this yt music playlist playing in the bg. the one that i linked earlier. by felix i think. it's been great so far. and i'm sure there are patterns here that π can pick up on how this music impacts my thinking. so in the future if i want to think a certain way and tell π that, it should create the perfect playlist to match my desire for how i want to think. <!-- beat:454 -->

^ an example of how meandering actually impacts the product! my mind is literally trying to show me what it thinks is valuable for me to fulfill my desires. i just have to trust it. ok maybe not trust it fully. my mind plays tricks on me all the time. it wants one thing, but presents it as something else to get me to actually do it. simple ex: i think a lot of my ambition stems from my sexual desire. like a monkey beating its chest or a peacock showing its colors, earning status and wealth seem to be my / and prob many humans' / way of pitching itself to mates. yet my mind would never want me to see that. so for many years it actually hid it from me. it made me think i was ambitious because i just wanted to "change the world for the better" or some other bullshit whereas in reality it was because im just a monkey that wants to have sex and i need to "win" whatever games the world presents because the winner gets the sex (not true - but it is conditioned bec i think it probably was true in earlier human species and def in monkeys and gorrillas and whatnot). once again an outdated craving. <!-- beat:455 -->

samsara. the mind. ok yea this is why trusting the mind is a stretch. i have no intention of trusting the mind because it is deeply conditioned over millions of years with completely outdated conditioning that does not apply to the realities of our current world. <!-- beat:456 -->

i am no longer a monkey in the forest swinging through trees. yet my mind is driven by many of the same impulses that a monkey's mind is driven by. should i "trust" that and follow it? or challenge it? when to do what? i have no idea. <!-- beat:457 -->

so yea trust is a strong word. at the same time, opposing it is not healthy either. the middle path. letting go of desires and impulses that are outdated, and paying attention to the subtler more beautiful kinds of desires and impulses. such as the kind to create. and even sex! i'm not here like some nikola tesla who says sex is sort of unnecessary or whatever. but there is an approach to it that can be beautiful. not course. subtle. artful. artistic. that's the thing. the best art in my eyes is the subtle kind. the coarse impulses and desires we share with monkeys and snakes and squirrels and whatnot. but the subtle ones. hmm... that feel more refined. more... elevated. there is certainly a notion of quality of desires. that is undeniable. and therefore much of my mind's natural movement will be wasteful because it is polluted by coarses impulses. but there are glimpses of the subtle. and so it's important for me to know which natural movements to allow, to pay attention to, and which ones to let go of. this is really important. <!-- beat:458 -->

that's the thing. there is definitely a notion of quality and skill to streaming. streaming is not just about following your mind blindly to wherever it wants you to go. not at all. there is judgement there. some trains you see emerging and you just say bye i'm not going to hop on. others you see are subtler and more beautiful and you hop on them with a smile on your face with a sense of wonder and curiosity to learn where it will take you. there are many kinds of trains. a power law of quality across the trains of thought. you must have self-control and wisdom to know which ones to hop on, and which ones to let flow by and dissolve. <!-- beat:459 -->

judgement. coming back to judgement for when to "launch" ai during a stream. ok, firstly, the more intelligence ai becomes, the better π will get at launching itself at the right time, in the right ways, to do the right things. but until then, or rather, as it is still learning and getting better, i will still need to exercise judgement on what π needs to tackle, and at what scope. good. let's think about this because this is perhaps the most important "dynamic" to understand to be able to design this system, this workflow, this "workspace". <!-- beat:460 -->

ok let's first also align on terminology here. what is this that i'm creating? actually fuck that, that doesn't matter. go with it. let it emerge. you are no one to dictate what it is you are creating because "you" are not doing anything. your job is to just witness. let it flow out. you are not the owner of what you create. you are simply a vessel through which creation flows through. which is why IP laws are dumb af – we need better ways for creators to capture some of the value that was enabled by them as a vessel, without allowing them to "own" what they create, which is just really really dumb. that's just an ego trip. <!-- beat:461 -->

ok easy to measure was bullshit. that's because "creating value" and "creating value that can be reflected tangibly as wealth, etc." are two entirely different things. there certainly is some overlap in some narrow ways, but by and large the overlap is not that great. and i certainly imply value in the former holistic sense, not in the fragmented, projected, narrow sense. let's talk about this gap for a sec. this is important to understand. <!-- beat:526 -->

also "humanity" is a vague term. by humanity i'm just talking about people. ofc one of those people is myself, but also other people. other individuals with their own consciousnesses, feelings, desires, sense of self, and whatnot. people here could be aliens as well, or other animals, or anyone. it's a broad definition. but ok that's also overcomplicating, let's just keep it to other human beings on earth for now lol. <!-- beat:527 -->

it should be obvious to any reasonably intelligent human being that there are many ways to create value for others, that they consider to be incredibly valuable, but does not involve a financial transaction of some sort and therefore does not reflect in financial wealth creation. <!-- beat:528 -->

ok but before that, even in just the holistic sense, what does it mean to "create value" for someone? <!-- beat:529 -->

"value" is something that a person feels. for example: if i give someone a hug and it makes them feel better, they would consider it valuable. if someone wants to get somewhere and i help them get there, they would consider that i create value for them bec i helped them fulfill a desire they had better than they could alone. <!-- beat:530 -->

so yea firstly the notion of how much value i created for someone depends on how valualbe THAT person considers what i did to be, not what i think of it myself. and it is reflected in feeling. like for example, someone who really really needs to take a dump and they have no place to take a dump and i happen to be running a store and even though letting them take a dump in my store is not allowed... ok that's a dumb train, basically my point is the value i create is independent of how much effort it takes me as well. in the rpevious train, if someone really really needs to take a dump and i give them a bathroom to take a dump in, i've created tremendous value for them, even though it wasn't any effort for me. <!-- beat:531 -->

so value is very contextual. it depends on the pains and desires and whatnot of the person and my ability to alleviate that for htem in some way to some degree. <!-- beat:532 -->

another important thing here, value does not require them to know that it was ME who created that value for them. that's another important point. when i walk to sightglass i often find this old granny watering a little garden she created i think that's on the sidewalk in front of her home. i love the garden. it's beautiful. and it brightens my walks. and seeing her care for it inspires me to care for things in my life. and we have a brief exchange - a smile, a "have a great day" or whatever and it makes me really really happy. ok i guess in this case she directly creates value for me. but imagine a little kid running around and stopping to admire the beauty of the garden. that kid doesn't know about the granny and the granny doesn't know about the kid. the kid gains value from the garden but has no idea who is responsible for it. yet they find it valuable. <!-- beat:533 -->

value. there is a clear diff between actual value and financial value. and we have to acknowledge that. BUT... things are trending in teh right direction. and i believe the gap between actual value and financial value will close to a much greater degree over the coming decades. if you're interested in why i think that, well... if you study personal stocks, network stocks, and rootnet itself, you will see a world a glimpse of a world in which many more forms of value creation will be reflected financially. you will see a world in which the granny i talked about earlier actually has a stake in the network nad is theerefore wealthy af.  <!-- beat:552 -->

ok. because i beleive that the gap between actual value and financial value will be bridged, i feel comfortable using financial value as the north star. with a few caveats... <!-- beat:553 -->

what are the caveats? i will only optimize for actual value creation. in other words, if there are 3 options in front of me... 3 paths to take. one clearly leads to the most financial value creation (GIVEN THE CURRENT FINANCIAL SYSTEM), but another leads to the most actual value creation (given a future financial system - the one we will evolve towars), then i will always choose the latter with the belief that sooner or later it will reflect as financial value creation even if it doesn't seem like it will today. <!-- beat:554 -->

there is obviously a risk here. and the risk is that maybe it takes so long for the financial system to evolve to reflect my actual value creation in financial terms before i die. and therefore, i will have created no financial value across my entire life even if i may have created some actual value. <!-- beat:555 -->

that is a risk. ok what do we do about it? well, let's not worry about it for now. things change so rapidly that i can start letting myself worry about this a decade down the line. but i'm a bit optimistic right now that the gap between actual and financial value will be bridged significnatly in the coming decade. let's see. <!-- beat:556 -->

but yea let's operate with that as the assumption: if the gap will be bridged... well ok no. something's not right here. i need to evolve with the world. i can't be so far ahead of it bec then the risk grows vv high that i will make no money across entire life. and i don't want that. but i want to be a few years ahead tho - in the sense that most people today should question whether my value creation approaches will trnaslate to financial value creation before i die, but tehre should be sufficient number of people who do believe it will and thus are willing to invest in me. <!-- beat:557 -->

comign back to siddhartha bro. siddhartha bro was incredibly wise. and one of his most famous unintuitive comments about the path come from the kalyanamitta sutta. someone (i think ananda) asks the buddha what is the role of friendship on the path to liberation? and i think ananda says is it like half the path? or some portion of the path? or somethign to that effect, and the buddha says no, friendship on the path is the entirety of the path. <!-- beat:565 -->

that is one of the msot profound things i think he said. let's understnad this. because friendship on the path feels like a separate, different, distinct goal from liberation. and yet he framed them as the same thing. if the 8fold path is to liberate the mind from suffering, and yet that same path can be described as friendship, waht gives? that's weird right? a sort of contradiction / illogical statement? but that's the thing. truth, reality is illogical. you can't confine it to one narrow projection - one narrowly defined goal statement. and i think this applies to my situation as well. <!-- beat:566 -->

see, even in my situation, even though our goal state is to maximize my personal stock valuation, doing that is the same thing as friendship with people who also want to maximize the value they create for humanity. why is that? let's think about this. basically what i'm saying is that the buddha's point that friendship on the path is the entirety of hte path - can be generalized to any and every single thing that we can aim for. it generalizes to any notion of "the path". <!-- beat:567 -->

let's take a simple example: becoming great at basketball. how do you approach that? is it by training? is it by practicing? sure. all of those things are required. but you know what you could also just do instead? make friends with, and spend all of your time with, others who are incredibly serious about becoming great at basketball. they are the same thing! <!-- beat:568 -->

because we are social creatures. we feed off of each other's energies. we are mimetic. if your squad is training, you are going to train hard. if you train hard, you inspire others in the squad to train hard. and then success / mastery is a natural byproduct of that friendship. of that sense of community. <!-- beat:569 -->

so yes. to maximize the value i create, all i have to do is optimize for friendship on the path to create value – with those who want to maximize the value they create. it's really that simple. walking the path and friendship on the path are two sides of the same coin. <!-- beat:570 -->

and i think π will figure this out. π will sooner or later realize that the most important thing it can do for me is to find people in the world who would vibe with my "shape", who i would vibe with, who are aligned in valuing the notion of maximizing the value we create for others. <!-- beat:571 -->

it's all really simple when you think of it this way. really really simple. <!-- beat:572 -->

and friendship on the path can be reflected in a very tangible way: bi-directional ownership of each other's personal stocks. <!-- beat:573 -->

think about it: if there is someone who i think is going to create a ton of value, i would LOVE for them to be on my own cap table because they will help me create value in ways that i can't even articulate. like how if you're lebron's teammate you're just going to get much better whether you like it or not. but sharing equity with someone in my stock doesn't reflect friendship because friendship has to go both ways. they have to also feel that i will go onto create great value, and thus similarly want to share equity in their stock with me. <!-- beat:574 -->

and in this arrangement there is a forcing function toward truth. if they ever feel like i'm not going to go onto create great value, they will naturally need to sell their stake in me and allocate it elsewhere - because i have become a liability to their journey to maximize the value they create. because by investing in someone else, they can actually create more value. and vice versa. <!-- beat:575 -->
