let's talk about decentralized social platform. was chatting with my friend abt this last night. we had a nice long phone call about this. was really cool to explore this idea. the timing is right. @signulll also recently had a few posts i think about how platforms (all kinds, not just social) are strongly disincentivized to allow agents to access / use them on behalf of people - bec naturally if they are monetizing through atention in any way (ads) then they certainly want people to expose their attention (i.e., rot their attention) on their platforms in order to make ad revenue. a really fucked up set of incentives btw. <!-- beat:676 -->

and if we zoom into just social platforms, it's clear that anger / fear capture attention far more intensely than anything else, incentivizing the kind of content that triggers these emotions. this is why ai doomsday posts will get like 1 - 50M views or whatever on x, whereas reasonable, optimistic, and likely true, takes on AI get like no engagement at all. <!-- beat:677 -->

in other words, exposing your mind to social media is exposing your attention - the most valuable asset you have - to utter garbage that will rot your mind and delude you. <!-- beat:678 -->

this is the sad state of social platforms. this is why consuming information through an ai that you control, that is aligned with you with what you want in life, is going to be how the best will want to consume information. <!-- beat:679 -->

and the idea is simple. i want to be able to offer my stream to others' agents - to subscribe to - and then peoples' agents will simply figure out what they should know from this stream and what they shouldn't based on what that person is interested in / that person's desires / that person's "shape". the truth is that for any person in the world, they wouldn't vibe iwth most / any of this stream. but if htey are interested in the parts that apply to them, they should just have their π subscribe to my stream and surface the right things at the right time - if they ever emerge. <!-- beat:680 -->

and as more people adopt this "protocol" of sorts, then we basically have a decentralized social "network". <!-- beat:681 -->

now there are obv costs involved here. and there is a question about who bears the cost / how that is managed, etc. like streaming content from me and having anyone fetch it from my site may lead to me burning a lot of money. putting that burden on people who want to subscribe to me restricts the number of people i can reach. so this needs to be thought through. but yea i think the timing is perfect for decentralization to enable agent-first everything. with the right incentives so that there are no "platforms" or middle men who want to prevent people from doing what they actually want to do. <!-- beat:682 -->

alright. just arrived at jandii for an hour ish sesh here before continuing our gentle journey through the city. streaming everywhere i go. got felix's album running again. let's get started... <!-- beat:683 -->

had a nice convo at sightglass mission before i left with a barista friend there. talked about a few things but what really stuck with me was the mutual realization that it's so easy to get lost chasing money, without really understanding what you're going to use it for or how it will translate into joy / fulfillment for yourself. <!-- beat:684 -->

i mean, there are many, so many, wealthy people in the world completely devoid of a sense of purpose. listless. you can find many of them in sf, working in big tech, making serious money but just... lacking a sort of vibrancy that comes with a sense of purpose. idk how to even describe it. but also, why am i talking about others? i have enough shit to deal with about myself. i'm not better than these tech bros. lol. can feel that condescension coming through me when talking about rich people who have no sense of purpose. i think i do feel a sort of pity for them. maybe even looking down on them - which is def not something i'm proud of. i guess there's a feelign of looking down because i notice a sense of arrogance sometimes. people who get paid mils to do basic shit not realizing that they're getting paid whta they are due to supply demand not because they're some incredible talent. but who am i to judge? am i talented? i think not. at least not yet. maybe one day. so yea, that's a key principle: focus on my own faults not others. there's enough bullshit within myself that is enough for many lifetimes to focus on without having time for anything else. this also ties into noble speech. is criticizing others' ways of life noble speech? right speech? i think not. good. ok, this leads to another π observer: one that checks for noble speech. this is really powerful. (π - set up this observer. and for noble speech you can just start with what i've talked about it so far, and use thanissaro's essay(s) on noble speech as well as context - but you have to apply it to the present context. if you take ancietn texts too literally and ignore this new technology and world paradigm, then it may become too restrictive? like this entire streaming may not fall into noble speech. actually that is another convo - think deeply about what we should publish from this stream while still adhering to noble speech. we have a clear objective, and so anything we publish must serve that, and perhaps what doesn't serve that falls outside of noble speech? i'm not sure, think through this. this is a sort of deep research thing i want you to do, and let's chat about it).  <!-- beat:685 -->

ok. where was i? lool. remember, that question does not matter. it's the wrong question. the right question is: where am i? that's all that matters. the present. perceive what exists in the mind right now. the current mental contents. the current tugs. the current sensations. the current imaginations, thinking, etc. what exists right here? right now? <!-- beat:686 -->

attention. i want to explore how π grabs attention. no, let's go more fundamental: let's talk about inputs itself. part of the input is π trying to tell me about something / asking me to do something or whatever. all of that is still just information that is coming "into" my consciousness. so let's talk purely about input. <!-- beat:687 -->

there are many ways to input information to my consciousness. one of which we've already talked about: a push notification of sorts right in the middle of the workspace when π determines it needs to do that for whatever reason (it will be the judge of that). <!-- beat:688 -->

but there are many layers to this. many degrees to it. like if π needs my attention asap in an intense way, it can literally block the entire screen and show me something that i have to address before continuing. sometimes there can just be a subtle hint on the screensomewhere. sometimes it can be a temporary toast or something that's like super super subtle. and other times it's in a different view that i only consume when i actively open it up to consume something. and some aren't even shown to me unless i ask for it? like there are so many levels to this. many degrees to this. and π has to have judgement about how to present what to me when. that is not my job. π will be intelligent enough to figure that out. <!-- beat:689 -->

interesitng. ok. feeling like listening tos omething else. felix bro is geting a bit old. been basically playing this album on loop for my work seshes over past day or so. time to change things up. what am i feeling? hmm... what am i feeling? maybe silence? perhaps. or nils frahm. alright, just started: [https://music.youtube.com/watch?v=UWKtCfbCGHM&si=EUAQSJMthNfEtjAR&drli=1](https://music.youtube.com/watch?v=UWKtCfbCGHM&si=EUAQSJMthNfEtjAR&drli=1). seems solid. let's see how this feels... <!-- beat:690 -->

ok. where am i? inputs. inputs. inputs. what are my inputs. i like thinking in terms of inputs because filtering messages that come to me, finding interesting things that i'd vibe with, asking for feedback on a task, or permission for something, etc. ar all just different forms of inputs. and π just manages all of my inputs with good judgement to optimize for our goal. that's it. really simple. <!-- beat:691 -->

my instinct right now is to visualize that ux. to visualize the "inbox" view. to visualize how it's going to grab my attention through notifications, etc. but is that really my job? it might be today, but soon i will not even have to think about things in this way - π will just be able to figure out the right ux given everything i've said so far. maybe i should just leave it at this. this is a high level prompt of sorts, and i'm going to leave it to π to figure out what the ux is to present information to me. <!-- beat:692 -->

ok so we've covered inputs, stream, actions. are these the primitives? no we have some more. there is a notion of "files" in this system. files. like markdown files, code for things like my website, etc. files are another source of truth. they are not simply linear transformations of things expressed in this stream. they are ideas that may have been developed and shaped in order for presentation to the world, or documents that capture key insights / things i want to happen that we keep track of. like for example, the principles doc is a doc. it is something we keep adding to, keep crafting as we evolve our understanding of how to best conduct ourselves to achieve the things we want.  <!-- beat:693 -->

ok nice. good. another thought on what data is captured: we need to capture what i'm listening to when i'm streaming. we need to capture timestamps. we need this information. this is all important. it's all connected. i can imagine af future when π just automatically adjusts my music to help me get into states of mind that i want to - that enrich the current train of thought. <!-- beat:694 -->

i want to surrender choice. i think recently sama talked about how it's not healthy to surrender agency to ai. i may have missed his nuance if he talked more about it, but this stance in and of itself is one that i strongly disagree with. the point is not to give up agency entirely - i would never do that - but to give up agency for all the shit i don't want to waste time thinknig about! what to wear. what to listen to. all the minute decisions involved to bring something to life that are liek far less difficult than the higher level desires. i don't want to have agency / decision making for 99% of the things that i spend time making decisions on today. maybe we are aligned bec. ofc it's difficult to know what someone truly intended wiht a string of words, or what the broader context was that led him to say that, but yea i don't want agency for most things. <!-- beat:695 -->

this is actually a key design principle. if we see the way this workflow is shaping up, i will increasingly make fewer and fewer choices. and even for what to express / work on. as my mind operates more naturally, even the need to actively think about noble speech etc. will dissolve because the natural mind naturally adheres to noble speech, is naturally at peace, is naturally full of compassion. that's another belief i have. the more i lean into the natural mind, the less effort i need to exert to keep the mind on a healthy track. it will just be on it. this means progressively i want to give up control. i want to surrender to the natural intelligence of my natural mind, and ai for everything else. i want to be a witness to things just unfolding through me. <!-- beat:696 -->

this reminds me of the mahabharata. of the bhagavad gita - a chapter / section within it. krishna instruct arjuan to surrender ownership to the results: to surrender victory and defeat, and to simply focus on the doing his job in the present moment. to surrender. that is a key part of krishna's guidance. to allow things to naturally manifest. swadharma - one's innate, natural nature. ok we have to talk about this word "dharma" and "swadharma" because people have misinterpreted this to such a degree that it's now seen as a part of a dogmatic formal religion - which is absolutely bullshit. <!-- beat:697 -->

dharma has many meanings. but one of the earliest and most applicable meaning is "nature". the natural way of things. the laws of nature. when the buddha talks about dhamma (the pali version of dharma) - this is usually what he is talking about. to understand dharma is to understand the nature of things - of oneself, etc. and to be aligned with dharma is to be aligned with nature. to protect dharma is to protect nature. not in the narrow sense of trees and whatnot, but in the most fundamental sense: in the natural way that things want to happen, want to manifest. <!-- beat:698 -->

adharma is that which is unnatural. but people have literally polluted this word so much that in many parts of india if you say "dharma" people literally interpret that word to mean "religion" and that too a specific form of religion - one that is associated with saffron colors and various sets of dogmas and practices and rituals and whatnot. <!-- beat:699 -->

the buddha practiced no rituals. there is no room for rituals on the path to truth - on the path to understand dharma: reality as it is, not the way you want it to be. <!-- beat:700 -->

which means "swadharma" literally just means ones own nature. this word has also been hijacked to convince people to do things that they are not actually designed to do. this word has been used to oppress people. to guilt them into doing what someone else wants them to do (benefits from them doing) onto people. it's fucked up. <!-- beat:701 -->

when i think of swadharma - the true meaning of it - i am thinking along the lines of the daoist "natural self" / "pu". that is what swadharma means. ones own nature. ones unique shape of self. the natural way in which ones' being / mind moves. that is swadharma. <!-- beat:702 -->

and therefore, the line: better to practice one's own dharma, than anothers is quite profound. the gita and the mahabharata in general is fundamentally a story about doing whatever it takes to protect dharma - both external and internal (swadharma). to ensure that you act according to the ways that your being naturally wants to act. to not play games others have created if it is unnatural to you. that is what it means to protect your swadharma. and this is really important for me. <!-- beat:703 -->

better to be killed while aligned with my swadharma, than to live according to someone else's dharma. – this is a core belief i have. this is why i never ever ever will allow myself to work for someone else. to me that is a sin. i will never report to someone. i will never let someone dictate what i must do - because it is certainly true that what someone else wants me to do is not the same as what my natural self wants to do - the way my mind naturally wants to move. <!-- beat:704 -->

this does not mean i don't want to collaborate. i certainly do. my personal stock is to help me collaborate. i would love people to give me feedback tell me that there are better ways to do something - but you see who the boss is there? truth. not a person. truth is the only boss i want to have. if there is a better way to do something, then i want to be pushed in that direction because i want to be aligned with "dharma" (which is what that means). and i want to incentivize people to help push me toward dharma - toward objectively better ways of doing things to achieve my goals. that is super important. <!-- beat:705 -->

ok. where am i now? i think my mind's a bit excited with that last train of thought lol. i feel strongly about not letting someone else cramp my swadharma. protecting my swadharma is one of my highest priorities. and i think it's perfectly aligned with maximizing the value i create! thats' the other thing. as i've talked about in the "personal stack" essay - ai will eat everything that does not have space for our uniqueness. to lean into our uniqueness means to protect and cultivate our swadharma - our natural way of being. it's all aligned. nothing is in contradiction here. leaning into my swadharma, protecting it, is precisely what will lead me to maximizing the value i create. it is precisely what will maximize my personal stock's valuation, what will enable me to form great friendships on this path. it's all aligned. beautiful. <!-- beat:706 -->

ok there was a thread earlier about hwo chats are lower level abstractions that i don't want to directly engage with unless i have to. this means the way π engages with me about open threads will not feel like opening up multiple chats and responding to it in each chat that it needs my attention on. it means that there is like a higher order "chat" or whatever where π just extracts what it needs from me, and then maps it to all the active / pending "chats" / "actions" / "threads" - or however it wants to organize things. <!-- beat:707 -->

that leads to a clear sort of design here. wow. i think it's actually just super super simple! damn. woah. π would actually just engage with me WITHIN the same stream of consciousness. there is no need to have a separate view or whatever. <!-- beat:708 -->

look... it's really simple. firstly if π needs my attention right away because it feels that it is more important than my current stream (or maybe it sees i paused and decides it's the time to jump in), π can litearlly just plop its message right here! like for example i'm typing this, and i see π's message right below this para emerge with something it wants me to do. as i keep typing this para it keeps pushing π's message down - so basically think of each para as a "line" in a text editor, and so π places its message in the line below this. <!-- beat:709 -->

or: lets say i have streamed enough and i want to start knocking out some of the open threads, i can just ask π. like i just do a π without instructions basically saying "hey, let's do whatever you think is the highest priority thing here". and it can then give me the highest priority task it would like to do and we chat right here in this stream itself! <!-- beat:710 -->

because remember, a core insight from earlier is that i am single threaded. if i am single threaded, why show me more than one thing at a time? ok maybe there are times when π is unsure what the right priority is, and so it can give me options right here, and i choose one and it then continues on that. cool. that makes sense. <!-- beat:711 -->

right? that's solid. this is actually really really good. damn, see what i said when i said let's not build and be patient. there is a simpler system here. if i had built that entire "workspace" shit that i thought was useful earlier, i wouldn't have arrived on this (or rather i would have arrived in this but much later). this is a great example for why shipping fast is not always better. like an artist, sometimes you have to sit with the paint and empty canvas for a while. until you can make sense of, and surrender to, the right feeling you have before you express on the canvas. reminds me of japanese calligraphy. like zen calligraphy. there was a video i saw once (maybe i'm not recalling correctly, but this is what i remember): a zen calligrapher was preparing himself to draw something on the canvas. he had his brush. the empty canvas stared back at him. but he waited. he wasn't thinking about what to draw, rather he was letting go of all preconceptions, all premeditations, to allow the natural to flow out of him without him imposing himself. he waited for a while. letting go. letting go. and eventually once the inspiration hit, he allowed his hand to guide him through the motion and express itself on the canvas - with the calligrapher as a witness to this process that naturally emerged from him to the canvas. you can find similar flow with zen archery. the archer pulls back the arrow and holds it, and lets go as they let go of themselves. this is also what i remember reading in eiji yoshikawa's musashi - when he was describing the way musashi approached various crafts and sought the common "way" lying beneath all of them - which relies on the same principle. <!-- beat:712 -->

ok coming back to the stream. π engages with me within the stream. that's it. super simple. and i can ask it questions right here mid stream! i can say π - what are the active actions right now that you are executing or whatever, and it presents the "command center" right here in front of me. <!-- beat:713 -->

and same with the browser! if i want to open up x.com. i just do it right here within the stream! and the browser instance opens up right here below this. and i can just browse it as i normally do and take notes right here. <!-- beat:714 -->

what if i want to open an artifact? what then? ok let's not get ahead of ourselves. there is excitement in the mind. let it settle. remember, excitement leads to delusion. you need a very balanced mind that is not attached to any of these things, that can perceive things objectively, as they are, in order to make the best decisions. what we have now is still not where it needs to be. there is something simpler here. be patient my friend. be like the zen archer, calligrapher, painter, potter, swordsman. when the right time arises, when you are at ease and engrossed and absorbed in the present without any attachment and can therefore evaluate that moment accurately when it arises, then you can strike. that is the way. be patient. let things emerge naturally. the way is not an imbalanced, exicted sort of expression. it is firmly grounded in the present reality. it is balanced. it is calm. that is the way. do not forget this. <!-- beat:715 -->

on that note, i would love to have something that tracks my heart rate and other biomarkers, and basically we have a π observer on that which pauses my stream if it sees that i have lost balance. the beauty of the mind body connection is that whatever manfiests in the mind is also felt in the body. mental imbalance has biomarkers. this connection was apparently actually one of siddhartha's core insights about how to liberate the mind. since all thinking all mental contents and actions can be "felt" in the body, by training the mind to be balanced toward these feelings, these sensations, you can then balance the mind without getting caught up in the thinking itself. <!-- beat:716 -->

this is also why i am not a fan of modern therapy. therapy seems to (and i could be vv ignorant about this because i have never gone to therapy), but therapy seems to try and process and resolve issues at the level of thinking. but when you are operating at the level of thinking, you can easily get caught up in the thinking. and in fact, you can actually reinforce the sense of self, you can reinforce the ego rather than let it go - basically amplifying your problems rather than reducing them. have you ever met someone who seems to get more egotistical as they go to more therapy? ok... i shouldn't be talking about others like this. i think that would break noble speech. noble thought. lol. "noble thought". whatever that means. the point is: i think it's really important to process things at the level of feeling, not thinking. the mometn you try to work with things at the level of thoughts, it's too high level and you can easily get caught up in the thoughts without seeing them objeectively. <!-- beat:717 -->

to view thinking objectively, to allow thoughts to arise and pass without latching onto them, you must be present at a vantage point that is outside of the thinking – which is precisely where "feeling" is. you cannot view something objectively if you are caught up in it. <!-- beat:718 -->

the ancients used the analogy of training the mind as viewing the turbuletn stream from atop a hill or a mountain. it's only when you view the turbulent mind from such a distance, can you hope to see it for what it is, can you hope to have a shot at letting things pass so that things actually settle. witness. do not lose yourself in the thinking. witness it. find the refuge within the self that creates the space between you and your thoughts, between you and even your feelings. witness. in buddha's language - just vinnana - just be aware. in other traditions: turiya - the witness state of consciousness. many words, many ways of approaching the same fundamental way. <!-- beat:719 -->

greats from across all cultures. all religions. everything - are aligned. they've just used different words, stories, analogies, explanations to point toward the same thing. and trust me, there are greats from all places and ways. that's something i've been realizing as i've consumed a wider range of art / expressions from all kinds of greats. actually, π - i want this to be a focus of my inputs. i want to consume more from the greats throughout history, throughout all cultures - who have found "the way". i want to understand them. their perspectives. i want to consume and appreciate their expressions. i think this will be a beautiful journey. <!-- beat:720 -->

ok let's talk about that sort of consumption. so let's say i'm at a moment when i'm ready to consume and i trigger π, and π proposes that one of the things i can consume is this feed that it's compiled about some of the greats. something that may take a few minutes of my time (or maybe i tell it that i want to only do this for a few mins). actually fuck that if i just get bored i'd stop lol, there's no need to constrain time or whatever. but yea then π would render a feed right below this and i would scroll that feed and whatnot. and it would be right here. within the stream. <!-- beat:721 -->

so this is good. the stream becomes literally my stream of consciousness. anyhting and everything is triggered through this stream right here. and this is the core (and only) surface! good. <!-- beat:722 -->

but what about artifacts? it would just show up in the feed itself right? why not? <!-- beat:723 -->

yea a "doc" opens up right here. like if π proposes we flesh an essay out to ship and i agree, π would just open it right below - the entire file, and i can edit the file within it, but i i feel like moving on, i'd just start typing right "after" it. that is beautiful. ok let's think about that. <!-- beat:724 -->

this means that there are just very few primitives here. ok jandii is closing. time to bounce. let's walk and think... <!-- beat:725 -->

ok. just got to sightglass on divi. i like this route. sightglass mission - jandii - sightglass divi, and then def a walk around the panhandle, and then back home (near 25th and kansas st). i wonder how much distance this is. we talking: walk down 23rd across gen hospital, take right on florida and left on 20th to get to first sightglass. then all the way down 20th, through dolo (the path across its edges, not cutting thru it) to 18th st, and then right on sanchez to get to jandii. and then from jandii keep going down sanchez, do a little wiggle and basically get on steiner, past reveille, up the hill and left on haight / page and across divi to get here, and then up page to the panhandle, go to the bball court, shoot some hoops with anyone if they're around, and then back the same route home. i'd like to know the distance and the elevations for this path. because i feel great walking this for what has been pretty consistent every single day for the past few days. i would come out to lower haight, but only this past week have i added the panhandle - basically ever since i met a friend in ggp i think last weekend, i've been pulled more toward nature and esp the panhandle walk which isn't so far away as ggp, but damn still gives me the nature vibes - it's so f'n beautiful. <!-- beat:726 -->

sf is a beautiful city. this is like my most favorite place. i'm a walker. i love nature. nothing beats sfbay i think. so much nature all around. and literally like a forest in the middle of the city - ggp area. omg. like how do you trump this? ok sure people can be a bit narrowly focused on tech but you can easily avoid those folks. i mean, i guess i'll be writing more about the convos i have in this stream and i think i can show through this the variance that sf has in terms of people and convos. it's not that hard. <!-- beat:727 -->

ok. let's get back to the flow here... noooo, no "getting back". wow, that's such a deep conditioning. to keep going back to something. to keep track of threads and whatnot. that's the muscle that developed for a pre ai world. that muscle is no longer needed - in the same way that the muscle to understand streets and routes is no longer neeeded after google maps. outdated. <!-- beat:728 -->

also some people like to complain that that makes us weaker / atrophies our brains or whatever. sure. if you don't challenge yourself in other ways. there are always challenges. being attached to a particular form of challenge isn't the way imo. elevate from one challenge that has been eaten by technology to a higher, better, more challenging challenge. that is the way. elevate. don't stagnate. let go of old skills. cultivate new. have good judgement about what skills will matter and which ones won't. that is the most challenging challenge of all perhaps. ;). <!-- beat:729 -->

a single threaded ux. i would never go open up the filesystem view. i would never go open up a chat. that is all lower level abstractions that i will not have to think about. or rather, i do not want to think about. in fact, we should measure every second my attention is spent on that shit and treat that as a top priority objective to minimize that. (π - think about how we can do this). <!-- beat:730 -->

i really like the way we talked about invoking π to show / ask / etc. like when i'm ready to get back to other threads that we started, i'll just start a new line and go π, and since there are no instructions, π has the freedom to determine what to show / ask / pursue. <!-- beat:731 -->

ok but this is an iterative process. how good will π be in the early days? well, well find out! start at higher level, more difficult tasks, and then if it can't hnadle, only then go lower. that is the way. else you won't test / know the boundaries of ai's capabilities. have faith in it. more than you have right now. and it will surprise you. (same is true for the world. have faith. be open. let it surprise you). <!-- beat:732 -->

so what are the primitives for this experience? <!-- beat:733 -->

also i want to build this streaming workflow by streaming. that is the way. <!-- beat:734 -->

anyway, what are the primitives? the output stream. inputs. actions. memory. hmm... let's think about this... what is there to think about? nothing really... primitives are simple. let's not intellectualize this. focus on what you actually need and let the "system" or "model" emerge naturally from that. approach this like a practical builder, not a theorist. this is a principle as well.- π. a really important principle. i think siddhartha developed an incredible theory of mind, theory of self, but that was not what he was interested. that was just something he needed to uncover on his path to liberate his mind from suffering. knowledge / models, etc. are byproducts from fulfilling your desire, from walking the path to fulfilling your desires. don't let developing theories themselves ever be the core desires. the core desires should be something practical. like this whole creating value - maximize personal stock valuation is good. it is practical. tangible. and naturally this will require me to understand how to leverage ai, which will require me to develop my own theory of mind, etc. but all of my explorations are grounded in the pursuit of fulfilling a tangible desire. that is what will provide the necessary forcing function. and such forcing functions are critical because it's really easy to get lost seeking knowledge for knowledge sake, losing sight of the purpose altogether. just like money. when you lose sight of why / the purpose / the desire you want to fulfill, you can get lost in the forest. the point is not theories. the point is maximizing the value i create. focus on that. tangible. clear. sharp. and therefore something that π can understand and help me with. <!-- beat:735 -->

i remember a convo with akshar in which he talked about how biological systems evolve to fulfill collective desires / objectives - something to that effect. i think. maybe i am remembering incorrectly. but that is quite aligned wiht what siddhartha talked about as well. "bhav": the core desire for becoming. the craving to "become" that leads to mind and matter arranging itself in a complex formation (i.e, the human condition) in order to fulfill that desire - which upon fulfilling gets even stronger (because fulfilling a desire conditions it more, rather than alleviates it). makes sense right? if you have sweet tooth, eating more sweets increases your craving (and frequence of cravings) for sweets. lol. <!-- beat:736 -->

ok the user experience here is a single stream. that's all it is. and some guardrails that π is aware of that ensures that i do this mindfully, balanced, and aligned with noble speech. so that i don't blabber. so that what i express stems from the desire to maximize the value i create. a simple game. ok let's get back to it. <!-- beat:737 -->

one thing i've learned is: whenever you feel proud of something, like how i'm feeling right now about the stream design - a single stream ux - you are becoming deluded. pride is a sign of attachment. becasue i am proud of this design, i am more likely to become oblivious to better designs for this workflow that may not actually be a single stream ux. the point is i don't know. and it's really important to recognize subtle excitement and pride and let it go because if i latch onto it, it clouds judgement. <!-- beat:738 -->

let's start with i don't know. i don't know what the right design here is. but ok let me look at this again. we have a stream that emerges from my mind (naturally, unforced). we have π - oh wait, π is not a primitive – we have actions that ai does. we have an underlying "state": a filesystem that contains artifacts, my personal stock state, and logs, and basically any and all information required to maximize the value i create - which is a crystal clear objective function that is clearly measured by the personal stock's valuation. this is it. these are the primitives. this is the game. legible enough to enable me and π to work together to make this happen. let's go. <!-- beat:739 -->

ok the single stream ux still feels right. ofc, i should be able to dig into the lower levels if need be. and i suspect that in the early days i wll need to do it more often - at least until π understands my movements well enough, and ai advances even further (hopefully this will happen soon! i still feel painfully restricted by whatever the frontier is). <!-- beat:740 -->

ok where am i? let me imagine the ux for the stream. let's say i'm streaming like i am right now, and i decide i want to scroll x. or i think of someone - say like ryo lu - and i want to go to their site and see if they posted something new. and generally just want to gently browse the internet naturally. what do i do then? i just do it through π! right? that's the most natural way. π is my "interface" to everything. π is the medium through which i act. alright. so what would that look like. i'd say π - i'm feeling like checking out ryo's site, let's go. or maybe even simpler: π - open ryo's site. and then it opens right below. like an actual browser instance thing with that single tab. <!-- beat:741 -->

ok good let's think about how to "browse". we have a v outdated notion of browsing. pre ai we've had to think in terms of tabs. there was a notion of "organizing" browser tabs. all of that is nonsense, outdated in the age of ai. how do we think about browsing from first principles. <!-- beat:742 -->

so let's just follow that example. when i instruct π that, it opens up a browser instance right below. it shows the url bar, it shows ryo's website. maybe i click a link and go read one of his posts there. let's say he talks about another company / person in that post and links to them. and i'm intrigued and click on that link. what happens then? remember, organization and the notion of multiple tabs should no longer be the level of abstraction i operate at. so what happens here? a new browser "instance" or "block" or wahtever you wnat to call it opens below the previous one, and the stream scrolls down so that that new block is the only one in my view. remember, there is no "going backwards" in a stream. you must stay fully present in the present moment. <!-- beat:743 -->

let's say i want to "go back" to ryo's page and read another essay. maybe i click on the "back" button on the new browser block, and it then opens up the prev page, but AS A NEW BLOCK below this block. <!-- beat:744 -->

what this does is that the stream of consciousness timeline is maintained accurately. we go in one direction. if i go back to someplace before, it shows up as a new thing. that is a good principle. opening something up is never "going back" - it is alwys going forward because every single moment is new and is to be approached with a fresh mind. <!-- beat:745 -->

there is an old proverb or somethign about how a man that dips in the river emerges a new man. the one who went in and the one who came out are not the same because time has passed and nothing is constant across time. and so this browsing being a constnatly "forward" moving experience is aligned with reality. good. <!-- beat:746 -->

ok. and then what about if i want to take notes? say i'm reading ryo's post and i have an idea that pops into my mind. or it is something that i am inspired by. i really like it. whatever! something is great about it. what then? how do i express this reaction to it? naturally i would want to write about it. let's say ryo has a line there that i really really vibe iwth, i would copy and paste it below that browsing block in quotes and just write whatever i feel about it!! <!-- beat:747 -->

but i must not scroll up. so how do i go back to that page? the stream is a one way flow. the point is to never have to scroll up... and let's not introduce any complexity here such as writing these notes in the margin to the right of the browsing block. becasue that is not reflective of the truth! they are not adjacent! when i am writing the note my attention is fully focused on the note, and not on the browsing block. so the note cannot appear at the same "leveL" as the block. it comes after it. naturally. which means i have to open up the browser again! i have to be like π - ryo's page. great! that is totally fine. in fact, that is exactly how it should be. now this stream can be set up so that it doesn't necessarily load that site again and just "renders" the same underlying tab in a new block again, but that is not my concern. in fact, for this mvp we should strictly NOT do that so that the implmenetation is also simple. only add complexity to the features or implementation or anything iff we need it, not prematurely. <!-- beat:748 -->

ok great. so we have a system emerging here. a stream of consciousness. can browse during the stream. can chat with π during the stream. can... what else? open files ofc in the stream: which opens up a doc viewer thing that i can scroll within that "block". good. makes sense. <!-- beat:749 -->

and every interaction / action i have within these blocks are tracked. naturally. part of the underlying log. <!-- beat:750 -->

and if i ever want to see that log, or what agents we have set up, what cron jobs are active or whatever, i just talk to π! and it will present that information in the way it needs to be presented! pretty simple... <!-- beat:751 -->

i should not have to waste a moment thinking about the organization of the underlying file system or whatever. or any implementation detail. or basically anything! just have to continue the stream naturally. whenever it's time to talk with π about open items that i need to unblock it on or whatever, i can do that. simple. <!-- beat:752 -->

ok but there is something missing here. the stream is good, but one of the things that the 8fold path reinforces is the notion of keeping the objective in mind. the easiest mistake to make on the path is to get complacent and get lost in "achievements" that aren't actually aligned with the actual goal. for example, one of the strongest warnings that masters give novices is to not get lost in the pleasure of deep meditative states - in the jhanas. <!-- beat:753 -->

i have to be similarly careful here! it's very easy to get lost in the noise. the objective is clear, but lets say i get excited and keep working on an artifact or whatever in ways that is actually misaligned with maximizing the long term value i create, i will have gotten lost in the same way that monks who get lost in jhana get lost. <!-- beat:754 -->

for siddhartha bro the goal was liberation. and therefore he was very cautious and mindful even during deep meditation to not latch onto the pleasure that arises from it, to keep his goal in site. that requires mindfulness. that requires understanding our progress toward our goal at all times. <!-- beat:755 -->

this reminds me of a great interview of kobe bryant. i think sometime in like '10 - maybe the finals w/ boston (could be wrong). lakers were up a couple games or something during the finals and during the q&a a reporter asked kobe why he wasn't happy / why he was so serious or something and kobe was like "is the job done? i don't think the job's done. what's there to be happy about?". this is the mindset that the wise stressed with the path to liberation as well. and this is the same mindset i need to have. <!-- beat:756 -->

so i think the user experience has to reinforce the objective at all times. let me think about the objective again because i think it's important to make it incredible precise. truthful. correct. because however we define it is how this entire system of me ai and whatnot will optimize toward. that makes this single objective sentence / prompt or whatever prob the most important string of words that we write. <!-- beat:757 -->

objective: to maximize the value i create. as i talked about earlier there is nuance here. maximizing the value i create is not the same as maximizing the financial value i create. we talked about how it's possible to create financial value while actually creating net negative true value. ok so let's actually understand this again. bec i'm realizing i haven't defined this well enough. <!-- beat:758 -->

actually let me take a step back. we know there is a good enough "mvp" objective statement to get started. i agree it's important but it's also a bit premature. let's assume we have X objcetive statement and that it somehwo loosely correlates with my personal stock valuation - which (ideally) will increasingly represent the value i will create for the world across my entire life. right? yea more or less (minus the 10 mil floor i reserve for myself). so that is loosely good enough. <!-- beat:759 -->

what that means is, in my workflow / app / workspace / whatever this is, i should always have my valuation in sight. that's the point. and i need to have enough liquidity for my shares such that my valuation adapts in real time based on my actions / streaming / whatever in the moment. <!-- beat:760 -->

becasue i see a future in which people will buy/sell my shares through their π's in realtime according to my stream. ok i'm getting carried away loool. before we think about that stuff, lets' get the early shareholders on board lol. this is like imagining building a palace without even having a design for it yet or having the stones for it yet. maybe not. idk. but slow down for a second. <!-- beat:761 -->

ok so we have the valuation somewhere constantly in view. and we have sufficient liquidity or whatever is required to encourage that valuation to reflect and adapt to the truth in realtime. that's it. how and what and all that will come later. but i'm just imagining the streaming and that is what i would like to see. <!-- beat:762 -->

ok it's time to ship something and start engaging with the world again. past few days have mostly kept this "stream" to myself lol. but it's time to make this more legible, put out into the world so that we can get feedback from reality. because even if creating valeu is the objective, and the personal stock valuation is the metric i am optimizing for, it first requires people to see this and understand this and be interested in buying my shares - before we even have a notion of "valuation" up and running. so let's start to optimize for that. <!-- beat:763 -->

how do we make this legible? if i think about this stream till now, it's really illegible haha. all over the place. etc. let's talk about shipping ideas to others. to the world. let's talk about making this process legible. without sufficient legibility you will not be able to attract shareholders / collaborators / support etc. - that i need in order to maximize the value i create. cool. <!-- beat:764 -->

ok back on my computer now. let's keep going. i'm feeling a nice session here. just ease into it. nothing special. just a natural progression of wherever we are right now.  <!-- beat:765 -->

ok. so the experience is quite clear. or rather, it's getting clear. a single stream in which it's just a single stream of consciousness. that's it. a single threaded operation. always moving forward. and everything needs to be adapted to this always moving forward experience of the stream. <!-- beat:766 -->

we explored how browsing would be "stream" like. new tab, opening link etc. adds a "block" below that is opened on that page that i can interact with. any notes i want to take i just type right after it and keep moving forward. it's really that simple. <!-- beat:767 -->

my mind feels a bit rushed. the more tired the mind is, the more it just wants to "wrap" it up. it's reaching. it's salivating for the completion of this thing. and that's precisely the wrong kind of mind to explore with. exploration needs to be balanced. if you lose the objectivity in the exploration, you are going to make mistakes. and i'm telling you making a mistake on designing this workflow is going to lead to potentially days if not weeks or months of wasted effort. you have to find the simplest approach here. and it's going to take some time. <!-- beat:768 -->

on that note, hit another 10.5k words today. around the same as yesterday. feels like this is the current stamina my mind has. around 10.5k words of streaming and it starts to get tired. ok. this is a good signal. this means i have a clear thing to optimize here. actually nope. that's the complete incorrect way to look at this. the number of words is utterly useless. in fact, i predict that as i get better at streaming - as the quality of words increases - which basically means as i can create more value streaming less words - i will stream less words. maybe. actually i have no idea. maybe 10 - 15k words is the natural limit for me per day and i just have to focus on improving the quality of those words so that i make more progress? actually i'm not sure. maybe this line of thinking doesn't matter. ok yea i'm actually tired lol. i think i only got around 6hrs of sleep last night? maybe around 6.5hrs. and i'm hungry! oh yea i'm v hungry! i walked like 10 miles today. ok no i walked 8 miles roughly. so about 12.8km. and i balled for like 30 mins - 3 half court games in the panhandle. and i hit some pushup after the game. oh and i actually ran a few suicides lol. felt in the mood. that added up. so i would say around 10mi of walking - exertion wise. i'm feeling like eating some more food, chilling and passing the f out. <!-- beat:769 -->

walking is incredible. walking through the city is a beautiful experience. nothing gets the mind going well like a nice walk through nature. this experience is showing me the importance of living close to nature and having access to long walks in nature. i think i would love to live in the sunset again for this reason. not outer, but inner sunset. around the start of ggp. can hit the panhandle whenever i feel like it and come to this side of the city. can also go deep into ggp and experience an even more natural nature less adulterated by human construction / activity etc. maybe even live in the mountains. a place i really like is north fork - where dhamma mahavana is. i remember spending i think a couple of months there back in 2019? yea 2019 start of jan - end of feb. so around a month and 3 quarters or whatever. it snowed then. driving was super tough lol. but the people there are so cool. i mean yea they have a lot of problems out there w.r.t. like making money - a not so great economic situation i guess given their expectations of the lives they want and the lack of economic opportunity? or maybe i'm not remembering it correctly. it's been so long. what i do remember though is some locals who were really into nature stuff. long hikes through the mountains - it is vv close to yosemite. i think the town is considered to be the foothills of yosemite? but yea that environment also feels really conducive to the shape of my expression. i work really well in nature. though i think i prefer sf inner sunset tho. i do want to be a stone's throw away from civilization and great cafes haha. sightglass on divi is really nice. i see myself going there on the regular. and also walking a bit further and basically accessing all the niceness of the human creations along haight, etc. while still having access to pristine nature in ggp. that is really the best of both worlds. going on nice slow jogs down to the beach. going on really long walks through nature. sitting down somewhere and streaming. that's a nice way to spend time. and ofc running into all sorts of folks throughout this journey. <!-- beat:770 -->

ran into M today near dolores park. M swings by dolo on wednesday evenings and really gets the folks going during pickup bball. he's like 50. from cambodia. but built really well - like he's super fit and athletic. not in a bulky way. he takes care of his health. really cool dude. anyway, ran into him on my walk back today on 18th and dolores. and we ended up walking together till like 20th and south van ness or so at which point we parted ways. had a nice conversation about surprisingly all sorts of things. our lives. health. the buddha and vipassana (he's buddhist and this came up when he asked about whether i live with housemates and how i met them - and i talked about how we met through vipassana like almost a decade ago and then he talked about being buddhist, which makes sense i think that's common in cambodia?). but yea a really nice conversation. oh and he's like he sees me walking around all the time. multiple people have told me that they've seen me walking around the city. i run into E almost every single day in the mission somewhere - either on 20th, or valencia, or on 18th near bi-rite. there's really a neighborhood / "village" vibe here. kind of nice. and like i think i mentioned earlier - had a nice chat with F today at sightglass in the mission. i find that conversations like these with people i know and get to keep running into is really special. very natural. almost as if i'm deeply conditioned to benefit from such interactions. which makes a lot of sense. these sorts of interactions is after all what we've evolved to do. <!-- beat:771 -->

ok coming back to my hunger. i'm hungry. what should i eat? or should i not eat? nah i should eat. i don't think i've had enough food for the day. i also had a big dump after i got back home after i ate, so i actually feel empty. that's the thing about massive dumps. they leave you feeling hungry. sigh... also i weight like 188 lbs now. that's a huge improvement. it's been a while since i've been this weight. it's a really good weight for me. i'm like 6'2" about, and so about 185 - 188 is perfect for me - given the weight is from muscle not fat. i got fat over the years a bit. some chub. not too bad. but definitely some chub. i'm going to get rid of it prob by the end of the year. making good progress so far. and guess what the reason is? being natural. walking naturally. eating natural foods. just natural. the best protocols for health are ones that are really easy to stick to. for me that is walking and doing some squats and pushups at the park, running a quick pickup game here and there, and just moving. it's how i think. and it's turning into my means to get fit. nice. once again the point is to not do "something special". i don't want to go to gyms and do some rigid fixed routines to get strong. i want strength and health to be byproducts of natural movement. natural movement of the mind, and natural movement of the body. and they're deeply connected. that's all it takes. have pleasant interactions with pleasant people. keep moving around. make friends. stream. work with π to maximize the value i create. and just keep moving forward. this is life. this is what it means to stream. this is how i want to move. <!-- beat:772 -->

oh yea, food. what's a good food? what should i eat? and yea i do want to eat. ok, maybe that el farolito place on alabama and 24th lol. that place is bomb. that quesadilla with jalapenos is bomb. let's see. i guess just move and the right thing will just come to mind and we'll just go there. no need to plan. <!-- beat:773 -->

hmm... anything else to think through before i think i'll wrap up streaming for the day? oh yea i would like a mini backpack. i was carrying around my keyboard today and a notebook and my hoodie and had all sorts of shit in my pocket: airpods, fountain pen, small ink bottle, keys, phone – like there's a few things going on here. would be super nice to have a tiny tiny backpack that can fit just the essentials that i can just take with me easily on my walks... while i was walking on valencia today i saw this dude with this really really nice tiny backpack. i think the brand was... something sproul? spriley? sproley? osprey!!! that's it. i just googled sproley and osprey showed up. lol. https://www.osprey.com/raptor-lt-12-w-res?size=O%2FS&color=Black - this is the one i think. this would be dope. something small. easy to carry. that doesn't feel like i'm carrying something massive on my back. <!-- beat:774 -->

anyway. this is the end of my stream for the day. we'll see where the mind wants to pick up from tomorrow. but yes i think we're getting really close to shipping the right mvp. the design is getting simpler by the day. just a little bit more to go is my intuition. we're almost there (at the starting point, not anything further than that!). godspeed. <!-- beat:775 -->

also the stream is getting progressively longer since i started a few days ago (13th). showing that i'm getting more comfortable streaming. that i'm getting more comfortable leaning into this process. and yea it does feel easier. i'm not fighting it. not resisting as much as i did when i started. just keep going naturally. <!-- beat:776 -->

C, A, and F, and i cracked up. that was hilarious. and for a moment i actually felt that i betrayed them haha. <!-- beat:779 -->

anyway, so yea sitting here at hedge. typing away on my magic keyboard htat i connected to my phone wired today. for some reason the wireless pairing is a bitch. not working. sometimes have to wait for minutes for the phone to recognize the keyboard. but yea, this is good. anyway, where am i? where am i? <!-- beat:780 -->

well firstly i want to say that what feels like a bummer is usually just reality pushing you in an interesting direction. my fav cafe was closed, so i came to another great cafe and got a chance to catch up with folks here. also A is a competitive barista and recently took a trip to florida to compete and described his experience there. showed me a really cool video of him making his cup there. A is from china and has spent years honing is espresso craft. i think he's one of the highest ranked baristas out there? famous in the barista circles. A reminds me of musashi. he's so serious about his craft. he wants to be great. he wants to do beautiful work. and there are no wasted movements. that's the other thing. when you are serious about something, one of the first things you have to do is cut out all of the other things in life that don't contribute toward that thing you want to be great at. attention is scarce and it cannot be scattered in an unfragmented way if you want to penetrate and discover the way. in anything. <!-- beat:781 -->

ok but does that mean what i am doing is scattered? look at this stream. my mind goes to all sorts of things. one could look at this and think that i am a scattered distracted person who is not on track to discover the subtle ways. and i thought the same! for most of my life i've prevented my mind from following its natural pattern because i thought it was like adhd or something. as a result, i suppressed my mind's natural movements. i oppressed myself. but here's the thing, as i stream i am realizing that even though my tangents seem like distractions and whatnot, they really aren't. i feel like i'm just taking a nonlinear approach. ok let's actually think about this for a second. linear vs. nonlinear | focused vs. distracted / scattered. these are different dimensions. different axes, and it's easy to conflate them and lose sight of the truth. <!-- beat:782 -->

scattered is to do different things in a way that is disconnected. such that doing one thing does not (or barely) benefits the other things you do. for ex: if lebron james started to, idk, play pool or some other activity that didn't benefit his bball, you could say to some degree he's unfocused. but if lebron is, say, doing some intense sort of alternate activity but it directly improves his bball skill - you would say that is focused, but nonlinear. <!-- beat:783 -->

ok maybe that was a bad example... but the point is that you can be v focsued and nonlinear. loook at musashi. the dude picked up all sorts of arts but progress in any of them led to progress in all of them because he was operating at the more fundametnal way that applied across all of them. <!-- beat:784 -->

ok let's talk about superposition bec. to understand what i'm talking about here we need to understand superposition. you can think of "domains" or "disciplines" as axes in a vv high dimensional vector space. and each activity / expression is a vector in this space. so let's say you make music and also design software. if you do it in a fragmented way, it means that when you make music, your actions are vectors that are very aligned with / along the music axis, and when you design, that action is along the design axis. clearly those actions don't impact each other because you are treating them as distinct separate things. <!-- beat:785 -->

similarly, you could make music in a way that uses your "design" mind and vice versa. such that those vectors are no longer on the axes, but are now in the superposition of these axes. like those actions would have a "design" component - its projection on the design axis, and a "music" projection on the music axis. when you act in this way, improving in anything helps you improve everything because you are doing them all in an unfragmented way. <!-- beat:786 -->

so when musashi had a breakthrough in pottery, he would feel that breakthrough across everything he did: painting, poetry, swordsmanship, etc. – acrosse everything, because he is operating in the superposition, not on the axes. <!-- beat:787 -->

the deeper principle: the more your actions fit neatly into a domain's box, the more it is confined to a domain, the more legible it is to a domain, the more you are acting along the axes - the more fragmented you are, the more disconnected you are from the superpositional space. <!-- beat:788 -->

and this leads to a really interesting tension: our world forces us to be legible to it if you watn to capture any value. so there is a forcing funciton toward legibility. but... if you become legible by discarding the superposition and just acting in a way that is neatly confined to a domain, then you have lost everything. you have lost the source of greatness. <!-- beat:789 -->

let's look at this a different way: the great polymaths were not polymaths, they were just operating in the superposition. to normal people who could not see the superposition - who could not see the common "way" across all that they did - would see these polymaths as doing "many" things, but from the polymath's vantage point they are not doing separate disconnected things but rather doing the same thing in different ways. <!-- beat:790 -->

and so even the notion of a "polymath" is rooted in delusion. a great polymath knows that there is no "poly" to their math. <!-- beat:791 -->

and this leads to another important truth: it is not easy to access superposition. i mean look at our world. we fight and kill each other over the dumbest things. religion? nationality? beliefs? dogmas? languages? all sorts of things! the wise know that these "views" are all fragmented. if you understand the truth of the human condition, of what we are, you will access the superposition and realize that we are all really, fundamentally, the same. a great hindu poet will vibe really deeply with a great sufi poet who will vibe deeply with a great christian poet. because they have accessed the superposition. from the lay audience's perspective - they view these people as quite disconnected, but from their vantage point they are more similar to each other than the many who identify with "islam", "hinduism", "christianity" who follow each of them respectively. <!-- beat:792 -->

we can find this delusion everywhere. if you look up the great poet kabir on wikipedia, he's classified under "hinduism" or some shit. if you talked to him he wouldn't have give an rats ass about any kind of dogmatic view. this is the tragedy. let's talk about "hinduism" bec. that's the religion that i was surrounded by predominantly growing up, so i feel like i have enough context to talk about it to some degree. <!-- beat:793 -->

there is no notion of "hinduism". this is a modern invention. today, all sorts of philosophies are categorized under hinduism. hinduism is basically pointing to an era of exploration and expression that took place in the indian subcontinent region over a few thousand years or so. it has all sorts of varying and often contradictory viewpoints. refering to hinduism as a concrete belief system is like clubbing all of the views in the world today under a single umbrella term "X". how crazy would that be. we know how varied the views of our world today are and it would be silly to club it all into a single "system". and yet that is almost certainly what our descendants will do! because it's difficult to sit and try to understand the nuance of things. <!-- beat:794 -->

if we look at all that is clubbed into "hinduism" - you will find atheism, you will find dualism, you will find oneness, you will find devotional singing, you will find meditation, you will find homosexuality, you will find using sex as a means to access the divine, you will find all sorts of things. you will find so many views that literally attack each other. <!-- beat:795 -->

adi shankaracharya apparently traveled through the lands meeting and discussing and debating with seekers from all sorts of backgrounds. he was a proponent of advaita - nonduality, that the individual and the ultimate are the same, and that the notion of the individual is an illusion / delusion. and so he met and engaged in discussion (apparently - who knows) with people with all sorts of views about the nature of the human condition, etc. i mean, just studying his travels and debates gives you a glimpse of the variety of beliefs people held back then. it's incredibly silly to call it all "hinduism". <!-- beat:796 -->

and the vedas. oh boy. so many people dogmatically hold onto them. there's so much bs in them! there's so much bs in all kinds of scriptures across religions from the past! many of these were written by regular people like you and me and for various reasons got popular and then were put on a pedestal not to be questioned. it's all silly. but yea you have all sorts of rituals in the vedas, and then you have many seekers in "hinduism" who say rituals do not liberate you and are distractions. which is hinduism? if you go to the mountains you will find ascetics living off of nearly nothing spending their time turning their attention inward and studying themselves and attempting to master their minds, and similarly you will find priests in temples chanting the scriptures as they pour milk onto a little monkey statue that represents a god in mythology. which is "hindu" in these? what is hinduism? <!-- beat:797 -->

and you can say the same about christianity or islam or anything! there is so much variance and nuance to it all. would you consider rumi - one of the greatest poets and seekers - a muslim? or something else? what was he? what was jesus? if jesus existed today would he consider himself a christian or look at it as some dogmatic delusion? what about mohammed? what would he think of what islam is today? i mean it's all so complicated and there's so much nuance to all of this, but we just like to club it all into these oversimplified buckets. <!-- beat:798 -->

coming back to hinduism... what is hinduism? it is a modern invention. an identity that was crafted probably in the last few decades or maybe centuries because there was a strong incentive to unify. that is the the thing. incentives. always look at the incentives. <!-- beat:799 -->

why did christianity evolve to condition its followers to conquer - sorry "civilize" - distance lands? was it because the regions that followed christianity built ships and guns and invented the notion of a joint-stock-company that enabled them to finance these conquering expeditions? – enabling the incentives to push them to do so? how did these technologies and instruments incentivize christianity to evolve to celebrate conquering distant cultures? and vice versa. it's like a cycle. new technology incentivizes certain stories which further incentivize evolving that technology, etc. everything is connected. <!-- beat:800 -->

ok so the point is that there's a lot of nuance everywhere. what is presented to be the truth is often not the truth. the truth resists simplicity - a beautiful quote by some author that my friend H shared with me one day - and i really vibe with this quote. i think it's true. <!-- beat:801 -->

ok where am i? i'm sitting at hedge coffee in the mission typing away. and we just explored some thoughts some trains that i have no idea how we got to. and it doesn't matter how i got to them. if i just follow the natural shape of the way my mind moves, i will naturally explore all sorts of things without really knowing why. just need to have faith that this nonlinear exploration will be useful. sometimes this kind of faith is really important. <!-- beat:802 -->

let's talk about faith. when you are trying to advance the frontier, having faith is critical. when the openai founders got together to build agi, do you think they knew the path? hell no. the transformer paper didn't even fucking come out yet! llms weren't even possible the way they needed to be in order to unlock this new behavior of working with ai. and yet they went at it. greg and ilya just went at it before even sam and others felt this is something big that requires their full attention. what did greg and ilya have in their hearts when they started grinding hard to build agi before it was possible? they had faith to some degree. they had to have felt somewhere deep down - "hey this could lead to something really important, let's keep going". without such faith in the process, how can one dedicate themselves so wholly toward a pursuit that is risky and precarious? it's impossible i'd think. <!-- beat:803 -->

siddhartha talks about faith as well. not faith in terms of bilndly believing something – he was famous for saying believe no one, no teaching, unless it is aligned with the truth of what you experience. the truth is the ultimate teacher. but faith is important. faith that you will somehow figure it out. that if you keep walking forward good things will happen. that things will work out. faith is warmth. faith is a subtle optimism. faith is surrendering to the action, to the effort. faith allows you to have sufficient ease to be able to keep walking the path even when you keep failing over and over again. <!-- beat:804 -->

i have faith. i have been failing in all sorts of ways across all sorts of ideas and things over the past, well more than a decade now i think, but i have faith. is this a delusion? perhaps. what do i have faith about? that i will figure something out. that i will discover something worth discovering. that i am capable. i have faith that this journey - whatever this journey is - will work out sooner or later. i have faith. and this lets me keep walking this path day in day out. the more i walk the less fear i have that things may not work out or whatever. the more i walk this path, the more i know there is no thing else i would want to do. whatever happens happens. but i have faith that something worthwhile will happen. and thus i keep walking. <!-- beat:805 -->

people without faith - people who cannot take leaps of faith - cannot do anything worthwhile. whether it's deciding to commit to someone in a relationship, or pursue a dream, or whatever - you need to take a leap of faith. there is no other way! if you are always waiting for the data to arrive so that you can clearly, logically, analytically conclude that it is the best thing to do – it's going to be too late! seriously. how do so many analytically minded people miss this simple logic? <!-- beat:806 -->

now, how would this person operate with knowledge of the company's true valuation, knowing nothing else – in order to maximize their returns? <!-- beat:827 -->

if you are a person who knows the true valuation, but you also see that people are really deluded about some truths about the world (some of which aren't even apparent right now maybe), and you think they're going to incorrectly value this company much more in like 6 months because they are riding this hype, then you may decide to actually buy more shares of this company despite its current valuation being greater than its true valuation. <!-- beat:829 -->

ok i'm now distracted. made eye contact with this beautiful woman at this cafe. you know, the kind of eye contact that is longer than the brief moment that it's "supposed to be". the one that signals that there's some sort of mutual attraction. ok maybe that signal isn't always accurate. but enough to act on and try to ask someone out. they may reject you. but i think this signal is enough to approach and try. i don't think i would ask someone out without even a subtle signal of mutual attraction. and i don't think i'm usually wrong about this. or rather, i've gotten better at it. usually when i ask someone out, it almost certainly leads to a date. ok that sounds arrogant. and maybe not even the right "strategy" because i actually don't ask out many people i may find attractive because i don't get the subtle signal that they are attracted to me. maybe if i asked more people out, then i would realize that there are even subtler signals that i've missed. anyway, why am i thinking about this? because i am distracted. i'm distracted by her beauty. by her presence. what is beauty? it's not just how someone looks. in fact i think that's a lesser factor. it's in how someone carries themselves. that's where most beauty lies. and there are many ways to carry yourself. what do i like in how someone carries themselves. i think it really comes down to how comfortable someone is with the ambiguity and chaos that comes with life. when i think of someone like siddhartha, they carry a sort of balance and ease no matter how challenging the situation is. that is beautiful. the ease and openness to accept reality as it is, not in a premeditated way. spontaneous. joyfully. openly. with ease. without tensing up the self in resistence to reality. that to me is beauty. simple is beautiful. no air of wanting to present yourself as somethign you are not. no air of wanting to show off. secure in what you have, in what you are. that is beautiful. that is to carry yourself beautifully. <!-- beat:879 -->

that is something i wish to carry. there is tension in the air. the tension of attraction. is it only in me? or in her as well? will i find out? probably not today. i've seen her before. this isn't the first time. and i will see her again. and maybe there will come a time when i spontaneously connect. maybe that time is today. i don't know. <!-- beat:880 -->

ok. coming to the stream. a single stream of consciousness. we have blocks. and we have a seamless interaction / mechanic for "entering" into a block that π has set up that follows the current thought, or ignoring it and moving on. (remember, this interaction - getting this right is super important). <!-- beat:881 -->

what else we got? what we have is my mind has not let go of the attraction. i'm going to let go of it now. this reminds me of the judgement that i talked about earlier. streaming requires strong judgement about what you want to pay attention to. this is not about becoming a slave to your thinking, but rather using your thinking to achieve what you want. this requires developing the skill of letting go of trains of thinking that you don't want, and leaning into the ones you do want. streaming well requires wielding attention well. they are connected. two sides of the same coin. <!-- beat:882 -->

ok. we can enter a block. operate within the block, and then move on from the block. this is the core sequence that i see. <!-- beat:883 -->
