ok what am i feeling rn? honestly, not much. but thats the thing, feeling like youre not feeling much is a sign of a blunted awareness. if you are perceptive, you will be able to be aware of a lot of feeling in this moment. not feeling anything is simply lack of awareness of what youre feeling. <!-- beat:1249 -->

means i need to sharpen my awareness. my sense of awareness rn cant pick up the subtle. its not perceptive. its dull. like trying to cut a tomato with a dull knife - just leads to squishing it into paste. <!-- beat:1250 -->

ok. lets play with a dull sense of attention. some moments are like this. the mind isnt always sharp and bright. sometimes it feels like jogging through a really muddy field. every step is effortful. feels like the mud is pulling you down. feels clunky. and it can be disappointing when you get attached to the feeling of gliding. but this is also the mind. every state comes and goes. the more attached you get, the more disappointed you get when it passes, and the longer you stay away from the states you actually want. <!-- beat:1251 -->

wanting is not the same as attachment or craving something. it's possible to want something without being attached to it, without craving it. if you dont get it, you arent perturbed. and yet you can want it and work toward it. <!-- beat:1252 -->

many wants weakly held. playing with wants and desires without getting tangled in them. the most liberated people still did so much work, they still had many wants and worked to fulfill them. they just werent attached to fulfilling them. <!-- beat:1253 -->

how does one have wants, work hard to fulfill them, and yet not be attached to fulfilling them? this whole process has so much nuance to it. and its so easy to lose it. complacency is a problem. the state of being at peace, at being liberated, is not a state of complacency. the point is not to become a vegetable. <!-- beat:1254 -->

actually lets talk about meditation for a second. im not a fan of this word bec its too broad. its like the phrase "starting a company" - i mean that could refer to your local pizza shop or the next frontier ai lab. there is too much variance, too broad, to be useful. and many comment on meditation without understanding that the label encompasses too much to be useful. <!-- beat:1255 -->

for ex, i remember a while ago when pc talked about how he has seen meditation make people complacent, dull even, and why he hasnt been drawn to it as a result. ok firstly its been years since i remember reading his post, and i could be completely butchering his point / the context. but anyway i remember nodding when reading it bec i have seen the same. it is incredibly easy and tempting to use meditation as a means to dull the spirit, to become complacent, to become <!-- beat:1256 -->

a vegetable. it is easy to use meditation as a heroin addict uses the needle - to dull the human spirit. and i think this is one of the most common failure modes. <!-- beat:1257 -->

and the masters have talked about this so much. texts are littered with so many warnings to not get lost in the pleasure, that the pleasure is not point of the practice. that dulling yourself is not the point of the practice. and if we see some of the best examples, the practice activated them, it did not dull them. <!-- beat:1258 -->

this siddhartha dude spent like 40 or so years after he was liberated ceaselessly teaching and traveling to all sorts of places to teach. i mean he prob worked harder than the hardest working startup founder - esp since he didnt waste a single moment or thought on something useless, bec he mastered the mind. <!-- beat:1259 -->

and he too warned against complacency. a key part of meditation / walking the path is to preserve the right tension. too lax and you cannot progress, and same with if youre too tense. there are many sayings to be watchful, heedful, cautious. the image of a great meditator is someone who is constantly aware cautious watchful almost paranoid if you will to not fall into the many traps of the mind, to not be ensnared to the attachment to pleasure or aversion to pain. its an active process that resembles, say, steph curry's mindset when playing: aware, sharp, active, alive - and yet not too tense as to restrict fluid movement and not too lax to not be able to move explosively. the middle path. <!-- beat:1260 -->

thanissaro bhikkhu talks about this well. i dont remember which essay but he basically talks about a sutta that emphasizes the sort of "fire" of the practice. it is a striving without craving. its v hard to describe in words. like swimming, you can only really understand it in the water. <!-- beat:1261 -->

and it takes a vv long period of time. i dont like 5 min meditation programs or whatever for the same reason i dont like a 5 min crash course on startups. you cant. and not only is it ineffective, it creates the wrong understanding of the depth of the practice. its no wonder many great teachers of the past wouldnt even talk to you unless you were dead serious about committing to it. the famous bodhidharma story illustrates this high bar well. <!-- beat:1262 -->

in the effort to make a practice accessible, its easy to lose the practice entirely. <!-- beat:1263 -->

i remember vaguely - i think peter norvig - was against bootcamps or basically the notion of becoming a software engineer in like a year or whatever. the framing itself creates delusions about what a software engineer is, and how to get great at the craft. <!-- beat:1264 -->

and besides you only get a glimpse of how fucked the mind is after days of observing what its up to. all of us are insane haha. and you become intimately familiar with your insanity the more you sit with the mind. and the more aware you are of your insanity, the less insane you are. thats the paradox. a truly insane person talking to themselves on the street with little awareness of the reality around them is not aware of their insanity, which is why they are insane. they consider their insane actions in the moment to be normal, which makes them out of touch with reality - ie, insanity. but if you are aware of your insanity, then as long as you are aware of it, conscious of it, you are not actually insane. the difference is in the awareness of what is insane and what is real. and meditation - when done correctly - is a way to become intimately familiar with our insanities.  <!-- beat:1265 -->

if you dont think you are insane, it just means youre not aware of it – making you insane! <!-- beat:1266 -->

ajahn chah says something beautiful about this. he says that every moment you are without sati (awareness), you are insane. <!-- beat:1267 -->

sanity is awareness, understanding of what is actually going on - the truth of things. <!-- beat:1268 -->

the more i sit the more of my bullshit i become aware of. and boy ive been so surprised by just how much bullshit i am haha. heaps of it. conditioned over millennia of evolution and whatnot. so many delusions about all sorts of things. and i can feel how much more delusions exist deeper within that will only become apparent as i dig deeper in the practice. <!-- beat:1269 -->

on this note, something came to mind and i lost it and i noticed a hint of annoyance. the practice is about letting that go. annoyance means i was attached to that idea. <!-- beat:1270 -->

oh yea. there is a beautiful paradox here. every moment when you feel deeply confident that you are right or whatever, without humility, is a moment you are insane. there is a different form of confidence that is grounded in humility, in understanding of the truth, that is not rooted in arrogance - the kind of confidence wise people have. <!-- beat:1271 -->

they are unshakeable. but they know just how fickle and difficult the mind is to guard. they are intimately aware of their insanities - making them incredibly grounded and sane. <!-- beat:1272 -->

but then you may ask - how could someone like siddhartha claim to have been liberated from all suffering, from all delusions (and thus all insanities)? isnt that arrogance that stems from delusion? the extreme position of liberation isnt bound by norms that we are used to. a truly liberated mind cannot lie about its liberation because it has no need to anymore, and it would share that fact if it felt it would benefit others. <!-- beat:1273 -->

the liberated mind is like a paradox. you cant reason about it. this is why questions like "does a liberated person have free will" are like just incorrect questions. the framing is wrong. first of all is there any notion of a "liberated individual"? thats like an oxymoron lol. theyve overcome the deepest delusion of an independent self, so how does the question of whether an individual has free will even apply to them? thats like asking if all of nature has free will? what does that even mean? meaningless. <!-- beat:1274 -->

ok. this is interesting. slower typing (bec im pecking away at my phone) leads to slower thinking. like my thinking is slowing down to match the speed at which i can express the thinking. this is a good way to slow down the mind. prob why writing with a fountain pen is so meditative. <!-- beat:1275 -->

some people think that typing / writing fast is important. that if they cant translate their ideas into something tangible quickly they lose out on something. once again this is an example of losing sight of what matters. it could be the case that typing slower leads to a slower more mindful form of expression that leads to higher quality ideas? in which case it would actually be better to go slower. <!-- beat:1276 -->

these kinds of situations exist everywhere. thats why latching onto one dimensional rigid things like "move fast" or whatever loses the essence. the best strategy is constantly changing! it does not stand still in the way a simple sentence does. you cannot capture the best strategy in words. or if you try, you  have to qualify everything with "sometimes" or whatever. <!-- beat:1277 -->

something really funny just happened. walked to the counter and D just handed me an americano and they were like "did you read my mind" lol. app they wanted to bring it over to me and i happened to go to the register at that exact time. lool. whatte timing. something nice about being friends with baristas. not for free drinks and whatnot - i think i end up actually paying more bec i tip more as a result - but the warmth of it. it feels like im at a friends / families place and theyre looking after me. feels like im a part of a larger community of fellow humans. <!-- beat:1280 -->

i think its good to be dependent on others. my parents often ask me to learn how to cook so that i dont have to depend on others for meals, and i push back saying that i actually DO want to depend on others for meals (and many other things)! ofc part of this response is a joke. im just a lazy ass coming up with excuses. but its also something i believe is beneficial. <!-- beat:1281 -->

depending on others connects you to others. i wont cook. ever. i will either buy a meal or go to someones home for food. and i will try to contribute something from my end. a great conversation, help in some other way, or if nothing else, at least money. <!-- beat:1282 -->

siddhartha had a reason to have monks beg for food. do you really think for such a trained group of people that they couldnt run their own kitchens? ofc they could. but begging for food is an important part of the path because it connects the monks and the lay communities around them. it humbles monks - bec asking for help is one of the most humbling actions: it reminds you that you are nothing on your own. it gives the lay communities an opportunity to serve others. and it gives the monks the opportunity to help the lay folks by sharing their wisdom and compassion. <!-- beat:1283 -->

there is a mutually dependent relationship here. the lay communities ideally benefit from interacting with the monks and nuns who are working hard to master their minds and cultivate inner peace. and the monks and nuns benefit from the lay communities who literally sustain their existence. <!-- beat:1284 -->

and if youve ever been given food when hungry by someone purely out of goodwill, you know how deep that hits. you cant help but feel grateful to such a depth that tears roll down your face. you cant help but feel connected to humanity. <!-- beat:1285 -->

but there is a skill, a way, of depending on others that is important to train. if you beg for food and someone shoos you away and you react with negativity, if you think theyre selfish or whatever, youve created a net negative impact on the world. <!-- beat:1286 -->

asking for help is to create an opportunity for connection. if the other person does not accept the opportunity, then you need to move on with positivity. you must think of them positively and let go and give someone else the opportunity. <!-- beat:1287 -->

ive asked many people for all kinds of help. whether for feedback, for intros, for a place to stay, to borrow some money, for food, for all sorts of things. and ofc ive had many responses. but in the cases when it worked out well - it was mutually beneficial. and it has led to a much deeper connection. <!-- beat:1288 -->

the unwise view those who ask them for help as beneath them. they feel superior in that interaction. they feel they have purely "given" without receiving. <!-- beat:1289 -->

the wise view requests for help as an opportunity to connect with someone on the same level as them. as a mutually beneficial, positive sum opportunity for mutual growth. <!-- beat:1290 -->

ask any great founder about who they have immense respect for and theyll tell you about the small set of people that stood by them, that helped them, when the world ignored them. when you do tough things, when you walk tough paths, you will find such small acts of help to be divine. you will treat such people as angels. you can never forget them. and you would give your life up to help them if it ever came down to that. thats how deep those connections become. <!-- beat:1291 -->

i want to always depend on others. i will actively not learn skills like cooking or making my own coffee or clothes or whatever so that i depend on those who do. so that i can connect with them. <!-- beat:1292 -->

another way i want to always depend on others is by being incredibly ambitious. the more ambitious you are, the more help and support you will need. the more ambitious you are, the less far you can go by yourself. look at the cap tables of the most valuable companies. the founders had no choice but to dilute themselves so much - they had no choice but to share equity with so many people - because their dreams are far too big to tackle alone.  <!-- beat:1293 -->

i want to be so ambitious that i will only have a tiny stake in my personal stock when i die. <!-- beat:1294 -->

ok. where am i? lets walk! orr... yea lets walk. when indecisive the first instinct is usually the right one. lfg. onward! <!-- beat:1295 -->

alright. i'm at reveille now in haight. decided to come here instead of sightglass divis bec that would close soon. no wifi here tho since weekend, and my hotspot sucks. so yea, guess just a stream for now and will see what we can do for now. <!-- beat:1297 -->

so ok. let's talk about π. the way it's set up. its architecture. organization. layers. etc. there are a few things to unpack here. and also there's that tool limit issue that i kept running into last night that needs to be fixed. chatting with π from the stream is janky to say the least. <!-- beat:1303 -->

so let me understand the primitives here: there are "messages" that i can send π from within the stream, and that π can send to me. there are "threads". a "thread" is a sequence of π's thinking / acting – which can include messages, but also actions (like π can decide to retrieve more beats for context or search the dir or internet or whatever). a thread is scoped to a single task. if the thread gets too long or whatever, it is compacted and continued in the same thread. threads can be "open" or "archived". this is important. when π feels like a thread is sufficiently done that we will not really return to (or if we do it is best done as a new thread), then π can archive a thread. there is also an "organization" thread that is running quite often to make sense of the open threads and determine if any need to be archived, determining their priorities, and determine when / if π needs to grab my attention about something in these open threads (which it would do by messaging me within my stream). <!-- beat:1304 -->

my flow is that i just message π whenever i want to engage with it. i should not have to keep track of the organization: mapping messages to threads, keeping track of open threads, and whatnot. i want to relieve myself of all organizational responsibilities so that i can stay true to the natural movement of my mind. <!-- beat:1305 -->

alright, so there are a few things that need to happen when i message π. firstly, whenever i message π in this stream, a thread is launched to determine: a) whether this is something that can be immediately executed (such as opening a link in a browser block) or requires π, b) whether this message starts a new thread or is a continuation of an existing open thread (and if so adding this message to that thread and hitting the model with that thread), c) what context this message requires – i.e., which beats this message requires to be sent as context to π + any other information it needs, d) what model is required for this thread (based on the perceived difficulty of the task). <!-- beat:1306 -->

and even after i message π and continue streaming, as long as there are active threads (unarchived), and as request are being sent to models and their responses are to arrive / arriving, there is an response thread that is running that is determining: a) can a thread be archived? b) priority ranking of each open thread, c) whether to pull my attention with a message in my stream or not, d) the message to show me (if it decides to pull my attention, this is the message shown, and if it doesn't, then this message is shown when i ask π "yo" / "what's open" or whatever – basically this message is locked and loaded for whenever it is right to grab my attention (that π determines / if i ask for it - which means i want it then). <!-- beat:1308 -->

i think of this as process_message(data), and organize_threads() - as these two meta functions. let's think about when they execute and how. process_message() clearly executes when i send a message to π. clear. simple. organize_threads() is executed every time a model call returns. that's it for now. down the line organize_threads() will also be run at the end of each beat i express. and maybe even when there is a pause in my typing. but for now let's keep this simple - organize_threads() is basically constructing the response_message - that is the correct, and best message to respond with given the current situation in the stream and all of the open threads. <!-- beat:1310 -->

over time, once this system has strong feedback from reality (personal stock is up and running with sufficient liquidity), and as ai really advances a few more leaps, and as there is more data in how i engage with π and how i stream, π will be able to figure out the right approach for all of these decisions - for this "processing" judgement. but for now, i will need to play a significant role in setting these "skills" up the right way. <!-- beat:1311 -->

this leads an important point about this architecture. there is only one "agent" here – π – that i know of and interact with. which entities π interacts with to get things done is really none of my business. i don't want that to become my business. whether those are other agents that π creates and manages, or agents others have created, or other people themselves. my mental model of this system is crystal clear: there is "me": natural intelligence – a stream of consciousness, sensations, feelings, thinking, etc. – and an artificial intelligence (π) that i interact with in the way i would interact with a person. i expect π to understand me better and better so that i have to say less and instruct less for it to do things the right way. and "right way" is not subjective. it is measured by the actual value i create for the world – which will be measured by my personal stock valuation (and other metrics perhaps but we're not there yet). <!-- beat:1312 -->

so these "functions" we saw above - like process_message() is a "function of π". that's it. it is a facet of π in the same way that "designing a product" is a face of me. a specific function. a skill. an action. whatever. but the point is that is not independent of π. that is not like "another" entity in my mental model. it's just π in a certain state of mind / being. that's it. keep it simple. basically there are two "entities" here: me and π / natural intelligence & artificial intelligence. each with their own understanding (model), memory, sense of self (not tangibly, but reflected in the way we communicate with each other - like i consider myself separate from π, and π considers itself separate from me - which is reflected in our back and forth exchanges). i don't want to consider π as a "person" or anything like that. i forget the word for this, like i don't want to anthropomorphize π. this is the mental model i want of this entire system. <!-- beat:1313 -->

ok for now these functions' prompts can be v high level. like for process_message(data) - it can simply be along the lines of look at active threads, and perhaps last 20 beats, and determine the action: new thread / add message to existing thead, and choose the model to use. and the model can be of 3 types: easy, med, hard - [sol med, astra high, and astra extra high]. that's it. we can adjust this later but this feels reasonable as a starting point – and actually execute that thread (i.e, make a request). actually i'd guess compacting would go here as well no? if it wants to add a message to an existing thread, but that thread's length is > MAX_TOKENS_FOR_THREAD, then it executes compact_thread(thread_id) and then runs that thread with this new message. right? <!-- beat:1316 -->

and then for the organize_threads() it can be really simple: for every active thread, on every message added / responded, the thread's title and summary are updated to reflect the latest. this can be done with a cheaper model like sol - should be good enough. having the right title and summary are useful since this becomes the "active thread index" that is used to prioritize. and then we have a prioritize() thing that is run that basically looks at all active threads and current stream beats (like recent 5) and determines the ranking of each, and also crafts the loaded_response "" - a string that is sent to me as message whenever it is time for π to grab my attention. <!-- beat:1317 -->

like right? this is simple. and i can inspect each of these functions - bec they are just artifacts in the dir. we can open and see and edit each and everyone and tweak them and whatnot. feels simple. straightforward enough. <!-- beat:1318 -->

ok so - we need to align on all of the functions required here. we have the two high level functions from what i can tell, we just need to flesh out the sub functions and the prompts and whatnot for each of them to get the full picture of this system. <!-- beat:1319 -->

ok what after that? well there's no need to think ahead! let's build this let's get chat to work and we just go naturally from there. <!-- beat:1320 -->

since i don't have internet rn, let me just stream some more - whatever feels natural - before i go and get π implemented. oh, and before that, i just remembered we have to align on and understand how π is represented in the directory. all of what we discussed goes in the π/ dir. self/π/. threads/active, threads/archived feels right. functions/, and each function is its own dir - makes sense. each thread can be a json file. basically everything in π's dir is not really meant to be human readable, except for functions - because those are basically prompts and whatnot that i see myself tweaking a lot. but even that, well i'm not sure what the standard is right now but we can just stick to that. not an issue. <!-- beat:1321 -->

so yea there is the underlying self/π/ that needs to be fleshed out as part of this architecture. and then we will have the complete picture we need! good. <!-- beat:1322 -->

and for the logging stuff - tokens used and model hit and whatnot shows up in self/π/. and the actual money that is flowing out of our bank account to the model providers and whatnot shows up in self/$. that is a good distinction. and then we can make the connections as we need, but this is how the information is organized. makes sense. <!-- beat:1323 -->

alright. should i address or let go of the frustration with pi re. the open threads? ok. lets dive into this frustration. there are two kinds of this frustration: the first kind of frustration is to do with π's inability to do things the right way, and therefore the urge to micromanage it by seeing what it's up to. the second kind of frustration stems from the frustration of the unknown. the frustration of simply not knowing how something works or what is going on. and these are independent to some degree. even with superintelligence, i may still have the second kind of frustration of not knowing how something works. <!-- beat:1389 -->

actually no i wouldn't. look at my mind throughout my life – have i ever truly been frustrated at not knowing how a car works or how my computer works or even how ai works? not really. i genuinely do not care how things work as long as i can get what i want seamlessly. it's only when i feel friction that i am forced to understand how something works to the degree i need to in order to fulfill my desire. i do not care about knowing for the sake of knowing. that is the pattern of my mind / desires that i can see across my life. <!-- beat:1390 -->

this is why i absolutely hated school / college etc. i hated it. i hated having to "learn" how something works without needing it to fulfill a desire. i'm only now starting to learn about how llms / ai works simply because i need some understanding in order to train my personal models (of my taste, judgement, etc.). otherwise i would not give a flying fuck. <!-- beat:1392 -->

i view knowledge as a tool to fulfill a desire, to serve a purpose. it is inherently useless in and of itself. this is why i vibe strongly with siddhartha – compared to the many other seekers of his time. siddhartha was disinterested in the many questions about the universe, the nature of existence, etc. that plagued many other seekers of his time. all he cared about was putting an end to suffering. whatever knowledge was required for that objective, he would gain it, while letting go of everything else. <!-- beat:1393 -->

i think this sort of focus is really important. once again it's so so easy to get lost in all sorts of things and forget the essence / the purpose itself. this isn't to say there isn't value in asking questions without purpose. i mean, look at this stream, the majority of my thinking i would say is exploring curiosities & feelings. but at some point a call has to be made about whether further exploration in a direction is worthwhile or not. because attention is scarce. if i went infinitely deep into anything, i could spend lifetimes on something as mundane as how to brew a great cup of coffee lol. judgement matters. having an objective matters. tbc, lots of nuance here. i've talked about earlier how achieving an objective requires not tensing up, not craving it, but actually letting it go. the paradox. it's all so difficult, so subtle. haha. so so subtle. everything. nothing is straightforward. nothing is "easy". simple is not the same as easy. simple is difficult. a line from one of the upanishads comes to mind: the path is as narrow as a razor's edge. that is a profound line. it's a delicate process. if you're excited you lose the way. if you're anxious, you lose the way. you need to be sufficiently balanced. if you crave reaching the end of the razor you lose the way - you fall off. damn, that's a really really good analogy. this is literally like walking on a razor's edge. if you crave reaching the end of the razor you lose the awareness of the moment you require to not fall off where you are. that's what i mean by having an objective, but not craving it, not imagining results and forgetting the reality of the moment that you have to work with in order to achieve those results. nuance nuance nuance. you can only learn how to walk the razor's edge by walking the razor's edge, by falling, by cutting yourself on accident, by picking yourself back up and by trying again. fall down 7, get up 8 vibes. alright. <!-- beat:1394 -->

but yea, this is why having a clear objective for this system is so important. esp as ai gets better. every single thing it does will be toward the aim of helping me create value - in a way that is reflected in my personal stock valuation. that's it. simple objective. it grounds the system in reality. it grounds decisions, and discussions and debates, in objective truth to a greater degree rather than just subjective vibes. ok that is a really really difficult task. if the prev para is easy to do that only means that i'm not aiming high enough, that my work is not difficult enough. so the point here is that – π will *always* fail to do that perfectly bec if it could, then it could replace me entirely. that's the thing. doing the prev para perfectly and surpassing my human potential are basically equivalent. if it can do that it can do this. this is a good working hypothesis i think. basically if i believe (which i do) that ai will never surpass human potential, then there will always be situations in which i will need visibility into how its organizing managing prioritizing threads and whatnot. always. if it's doing a great job then that's simply a signal that i need to become more ambitious and aim higher, and not to just "leave that layer to ai". maybe. or maybe... ok i think this intuition is wrong. here's why: <!-- beat:1402 -->

ok great. here's the thing. it is entirely possible for ai to manage all threads perfectly while still not replacing me. to understand why we need to approach this with the notion of "complexity". each thread has a certain complexity to it. the more complex a thread, the more "difficult" it is. let's just define complexity this way for now, but i do have a more tangible one for later. every thread that i spin up is essentially a "sub-thread" of the main thread to create value. that's it. right? every single thread that i start is a sub-thread of the main thread. and each sub-thread can have further sub-threads. now here is a rule that i think holds: the sum of complexities of sub-threads is superexponentially less complex than the complexity of the thread that they emerged from. yea. this is true. certainly this is true. i mean so many examples i can provide here, but not necessary atm. unpacking this is to unpack some of the deepest intuitions i have about everything. doesn't feel like the right time for that because it's irrelevant to the current work. <!-- beat:1404 -->

given that to be true, then there can certainly be scenarios where ai handles and manages all open threads perfectly, but is still unable to handle the highest order thread better than i can – from which all of the sub-threads emerge from. the world is actually really bad at this lol. our world conditions us to "finish" things as if there is something innately valuable about completing something. why? what is the point of completing something if you find out midway that it's not worth completing / that there's something better worth completing. i've thrown away countless ideas. heck i've thrown away a handful of startups i started bec i was like this is dumb i don't want to be spending a decade working on this problem that is just not an important problem to solve (or that ai will solve it someday) or whatever. if i didn't do that i would simply not be here right now and would still be building some outdated idea. letting go of the majority of threads is likely the right move. in fact, if our completion rate is too high it is a negative signal most likely because it means our rate of idea generation (i.e, our rate of thread generation) is too slow or that we are mis-prioritizing (i.e., we are unable to surface the underlying power law distribution in value of threads). imagine someone moving so fast that they uncover like 100 ideas every hour - it would have to be true that only 0.1% of those threads would be completed, otherwise they'd prob be wasting a ton of resources on many not great ideas and not have enough resources for the great ones when they emerge. <!-- beat:1423 -->

there is a key implicit principle with this stream that i've been working with in the way i've been building, which is: the stream itself becomes the data. like seeing the raw stream.md file should just show everything that happened. ok some things obv not - like π's data, etc. – but everything from MY end should just be in the stream! this is why even though i'm browsing normally, in the stream you see the trail that my attention took in its browsing session - all the sites it went through (and over time we will capture even more info). but yea, the point is stream needs to be all of my expressions. i want to work toward that. that feels natural. <!-- beat:1509 -->

and so if we have a react component here for the status, we have to design it in a way such that it preserves the original state and the set of transactions to that state that happened. for example: if the status block shows all open items ranked, and each item is like a button i can click that shows me the response from that thread, and say i click on the first item, the other items should not be removed, just the state updates the first item to "selected" (and ofc the state updates as a result with like a green border around the selected one, while the other options are dimmed). this way we know what was shown, and we know what was selected, from the way the component itself is designed. that is important because lit these components' states will be used to train the model π uses to prioritize threads! if i keep selecting the 3rd option or whatever, π will learn that that kind of stuff needs to be ranked higher. <!-- beat:1510 -->

so designing the stream components' state well feels really important. and this is a principle for building anything in the stream experience. this is also why i don't want stream and chat to be separate interfaces. by scrolling the stream i want to see when i stopped to invoke π, what i asked it, when i moved to something else, etc. i want the entire movement of my attention to be visible from this stream itself. i can't articulate why i feel this is important, but i feel it's really really important that we set this up this way. damn, an inexplainable intuition. there's something beautiful about it. it's like a lens into myself. into understanding the shape of my mind's movement. and i feel a desire to want to be able to see it in this stream form. that's exciting to me for some reason. idkwhy. <!-- beat:1511 -->

ok. let me settle into concentration, into balance while still prioritizing legibility. ok what do i mean by making this journey legible? by that i mean telling the story of this journey in a way that is easily understandable by others - esp by the right people (we will get to what this means) - so that they would want to support this exploration. support isnt the right word. so that they would want a stake in this exploration. i want people driven by the desire to create and capture value. i dont want to work with those who think they are driven by some purely altruistic feeling - they tend to be the most egotistical. funny paradox. i have never found greater sense of a superiority complex than those donating away money. its funny how even the act of giving, an opportunity to dissolve the ego, can actually do the exact opposite. and i notice this in myself! i feel better about myself if i donate money to some cause. i feel like im an enabler or whatever. and my ego gets inflated bec it thinks it is selfless in that action - bec it did it without getting anything in return. but in reality they did it to feel that ego, to get the status that comes from donating. how many would donate if no one else knew about their donations? i know some truly selfless folks and no one, absolutely no one, knows of their contributions. i would habe either if not for some crazy circumstance that led me to discovering what had happened. theyve donated skillfully - as a means to dissolve ego and gain peace - not to reinforce their sense of self. i feel like nonprofits are run by more egotistical folks than startups lool. its such a funny paradox. but this is a recurring observation. startup founders - at least the good ones - really have no choice but to take feedback from reality, they have no choice but to be humbled by reality - to become painfully aware of how utterly useless and incompetent they are most of the time. i just dont see that humility beaten into the nonprofit founders - they seem less humbled by reality, and more... well it doesnt matter. the point of this beat is to make sense of the people i want to connect with who would want a stake in this exploration. i want to work with people who care deeply about creating and capturing value. not about other stuff. i dont want to work with people who think they are altruistic angels from beyond. i want to work with humans who understand that they are purely selfish (like i am!) and acknowledge that and act on it. its just more honest. <!-- beat:1552 -->

anyway, legibility. where am i? need to make this exploration legible and recruit the right people to hold a stake in whatever value this exploration - my life - creates. thats what i want to do. and the sooner this exploration is legible, the better it is. <!-- beat:1553 -->

what is the most legible narrative for this exploration? ok so we have rootnet - a new kind of network with aligned incentives. we have a more natural way of working with ai (the streaming harness etc.), we have the training personal models of a persons taste that helps them scale themselves. we have the personal stock. these are the narratives i see. well, we have the meta one about what it means to be an individual in the age of ai - how the notion of an individual expands from just a person to now also include an ai that is aligned with, and understands, the person. <!-- beat:1554 -->

of these i think its imp to start at the individual level first. oh and i forgot another angle: a decentralized, agent first, social (and broadly all) platform. i mean that is what rootnet will become as well - rootnet will not just be about financial instruments (personal, network stocks), but also a new kind of internet itself - communication as well. <!-- beat:1555 -->

ok where am i? legibility... start small. thats the way. there are all of these ideas, but the higher order ideas dont carry weight without some lower order ones landing. what i mean is, for ex, creating a better way to work with ai - better workflow - that others also derive value from, will give a lot more weight to the higher order narrative of wanting to create value and using personal ai and my personal stock to help me do that. the latter is theoretical unless someone has a clear "oh this is clear evidence of ability to create value, and thus i can bet on this persons judgement". right? lower order, narrower, narratives landing is what gives you credibility to share the higher order ones. <!-- beat:1559 -->

so my hunch is that we can just start high. start with the narrative of the merge. the desire to basically become superhuman - by leveraging ai, by training models of the individual uniqueness, to scale your uniqueness. now this is an ambitious narrative with some solid meat on it to latch onto. makes sense. <!-- beat:1561 -->

can just establish this in a super legible way. legibility requires oversimplification. that is the hard truth. i mean ive discussed the notion of nuance so many times in this stream. how theres just so so much nuance to everything. but nuance does not make for a great story. its imp to understand why. <!-- beat:1562 -->

what makes a story great? what makes it legible? a great story is easy to understand. it presents desires very simply, in v narrow ways - but slowly peels them and shows the layers of nuance within, over time. for ex, the character arjuna in the mahabharata - i mean his story if you ask the ave person will be something really simple: something something revenge, avenge what happened to his wife, family, kingdom, etc. i mean a p simple revenge arc of sorts. a really simple aim. a simple premise: a war that he has to win. and thats about it! but as you get into the story, you see arjuna experience so so many emotions. the brave warrior can be seen on his knees gripped by anxiety and sadness. i mean there isnt an emotion he doesnt experience through the entire story. thats the thing - an actual persons life has so much nuance that a story cannot capture. but if you just dump the nuance at the start it becomes too difficult to understand. the premise must be really simple. the opening line / the arc of the story must be vv simple. like a 5 year old should get it. but as you go deeper, all the necessary nuance must exist - else the character remains one dimensional. <!-- beat:1563 -->

thats the thing. i need to present myself as a one dimensional character, while acknowledging the actual nuances to all of my desires and stances and presenting them tactfully, layered, at the right time to the right people in the right way. <!-- beat:1564 -->

good. this makes sense. and the funny thing here is, people hate nuance and yet criticize one dimensional presentations of ideas and people. its such a funny situation. if i present my nuance up front i will be unable to get attention from anyone. if i narrow my character down to a one dimensional narrative, i will get attention, but also a lot of hatred, anger etc, bec people will certainly misinterpret it. loool. funny. <!-- beat:1565 -->

another thought i had during the walk is that i will never reach out to anyone to pitch my personal stock. ever. i will strive to ship and create value, and place my personal stock prominently on my site as a means for someone who considers my exploration to be valuable to reach out and start a conversation about participating in my personal stock. this is a hard rule. why? otherwise i don't think you get a positive-sum relationship. the same dynamics apply for a company. founders and potential shareholders (employees / investors / anyone) need to meet in the middle. and esp for a new instrument like the personal stock. reaching out with the personal stock pitch will attract the wrong kinds of people. ok i'm doing a terrible job at explaining why. i think the feeling is crystal clear, but the explanation is lacking. this is an example of an intuition that hasn't been thought out yet. i'm not sure why i feel this strongly about this, but i do. and i think i'll explore why later. it's premature for that thinking. <!-- beat:1572 -->

i had this thought bec that legibility blurb i was crafting earlier had the pitch for the personal stock, but that gave me the ick. ew. why would i ever want to reach out and be like come participate in my personal stock. that has to happen naturally. i certainly want to share a stake in my success in life with those who can help me succeed, but that relationship has to blossom organically, naturally. i'm confident it'll happen one day, but it isn't something i want to optimize for directly. it's like the most beautiful things in life - you can't optimize for them directly because if you do, you lose it. like friendships. if you go to an event or something with the "goal" of making good friends or say to find the love of your life, you're going to pollute the process. you're forcing things. i strongly believe these things have to happen naturally. organically. they can't be polluted. if you try to force it, it actually becomes toxic. reminds me of a poem i read a while ago, not sure by who, about how loving someone is like holding water in the palm of your hand - if you grasp onto it, the water falls out of your hand. you can't latch onto it. you can't force it. if you do, you lose it. natural. organic. i think this is why i'm instinctively against pitching the personal stock. my personal stock is really important to me. i mean, sharing a stake in it is like sharing a stake in myself. i want my relationships with my shareholders to fulfilling, meaningful, that last long periods of time. i mean, these are going to be the people that help me realize my potential and share in my success. that's really fucking personal. such relationships can't be rushed. they must not be forced. <!-- beat:1573 -->

because the niece studied biomedical engineering (his field) but has decided not to pursue something technical and he's disappointed that she's not getting into something technical like him. i mean his disappointment is disappointing. i'm so glad she's not doing something technical if her heart is not in it. besides, i think the "technical" level is going to get eaten by ai first. technical people will be as in demand as, say, cobolt mainframe engineers are today. let's not push more people into a technical path if their heart is not aligned with it. and i've seen this happen so much. i did 4 years of high school in india, went to an engineering prep coaching thing for some dumb engineering college entrance exams that i never took. but man, so so so many people with so much talent in all sorts of things reduced to "engineering". such a sad sad sad thing. wow. so sad. anyway. i think our successors will look back at this pushing people into shit that they aren't aligned with as a cardinal sin - which it is imo. i mean knowing what i know now, i would much rather die than be forced to do something that is misaligned with my natural self. i mean i'd ofc try to run away into the forest or something, but if i were literally forced then i would rather die. it is self-harm to go against the natural self. to me it's no wonder why so many people are unhappy. how do you allow another human being to control your attention for you in exchange for money and be happy? your attention is the most valuable thing you have and you basically sell it to another person to use it how they want to fulfill their own desires? it's sad how normalized this is. look, if you absolutely need to do that in order to survive, do it. but we become addicted to it - the lifestyle, the money. they say that a monthly salary is the most addictive substance in the universe. more addicting than the most concentrated heroin. i mean, you literally wow. you just get addicted. you start eating fancy. you start living fancy. your lifestyle blows up. you treat that as the new baseline from which you must only rise and cannot fall. you become fragile. and the worst: you become ok with letting another person direct your attention. being employed by someone is like being their llm / ai agent. literally. if you think you are free to explore what you want in that relationship, then you cannot think freely. anyone with an ounce of free thinking will find that their natural movement is incredibly misaligned with anyone else's natural movement. meaning, the more free thinking you are, the more confined you will feel being employed by someone. i think naval had this banger tweet - once you taste freedom, you can never go back. it's too sweet. <!-- beat:1575 -->

my first instinct is to have a separate pane to see open threads, other information π wants to share with me, etc. all in one place, but separate from the stream. this is a terribly outdated instinct. once again this stems from years of pre-ai product intuition. it's sorely outdated. separating information into panes is not the way to go. everything needs to be in one continuous stream. any information i want must be within this experience right here. within this stream of consciousness. <!-- beat:1594 -->

at this moment, as i'm typing this, there are like a few open threads. i would bet they've all completed execution bec they were p simple tasks. and i don't even see the prev message bubbles bec they've left my field of view. good. and i'm not supposed to scroll up - this stream is meant to be a march forward, all the way till my death lol. so given these constraints, what needs to happen right here? <!-- beat:1595 -->

what needs to happen here? hmm... what needs to happen? that is the question. what do i want right here to manage my open threads? in fact, i've actually forgotten what my open threads are! GREAT! this is actually perfect because i have terrible memory tbh and i'm going to be in this situation vv often. like this is the default situation: i've messaged π to do various things and i've completely forgotten about them and i'm just streaming and coming up with more ideas / thinking / whatever. i'm just naturally going with the flow. so at this moment it is an unkonwn to me what is open behind the scenes. everything is unknown. on my screen right now is literally just a portion of the prev beat, and this beat. just text on a screen with a blinking cursor. that is it. nothing else is on screen - as it should be. let me first start with understanding the desire that may lead me to want to check in re. the threads. i'm not sure that desire would ever even emerge. like i would just keep going forgetting entirely about the open threads. hmm... but naturally there would come a point when i would want to know right? would it? i'm really not sure. wow. this is actually hilarious. but it's important that i'm reminded of the right open threads right? otherwise i wouldn't be able to ship anything to the world and my thinking would just go in circles - hitting the same tasks over and over again and never actually completing them. <!-- beat:1596 -->

ok good, so there is a subtle forcing function needed here that's basically like "yo karthik a previous version of you thought these things were important to do, and i've made progress on them and am ready to present them to you.". to which i go, "ok bro show me what you got", and it would show me and then i would remember the desires i had about them and then follow the strongest desire – basically choose the open thread i feel is most important and unblock π on shipping that thing. that's it. <!-- beat:1597 -->

ok so i'm open to being pulled back to shipping. my natural movement of my mind is to forget and to keep moving forward, exploring new trains of thoughts, ideas, and whatnot, completely forgetting about the old ones. but remembering the right old ones and seeing them through is how i actually ship my work to the world. and so this tension is required. this is the right tension – like the lute's right tension – that i actually need otherwise my thinking will just go on and on without feedback from the world. great. let's get π to complement me and create the tension to ship. <!-- beat:1598 -->

beautiful. an interface just came to my mind. look, we have a lot of margin space on the left and right (good! more spacing is something i like). and this gives us a really really clean way to show open threads - on the left or right margin. why margin and not in the stream? in the stream it needs to be invoked / asked by me. but the margin is the space where π can tug my attention. that's a good principle for designing this app. anything is stream only comes to stream if i explicitly ask for it. the stream itself is a sacred space of sorts meant to fully protect my natural movement of my mind. the margins are places where π can pull my attention in ways that help me create value (in this case, to actually complete open threads and ship things!). good. i really like this. <!-- beat:1599 -->

so what am i seeing here? actually this is really good. i like what i'm seeing. on the left margin i'm seeing a subtle not pronounced, sort of faded list of the open threads titles. as i'm typing as i'm launching new threads and as π is working on them, i see the titles updating and the order changing (acc to how π feels they should be prioritized). and this view is constantly on the left margin as i stream. it is a constant "tension" on my attention reminding me and nudging me to see tasks through so that we can ship things to the world. that's what it is – the tension to ship! <!-- beat:1600 -->

ok let's say we see like 10 open threads on the left. and we can even color the title based on what action is required. for example, if π is blocked and needs my input to proceed, it is orange. if there is some error, it is red. if it is executing, it is just gray (and there is some animation here that signals that π is actively doing something in that thread - it can be subtle, perhaps just a wave of light moving across the title from left to right). <!-- beat:1602 -->

this is good. now what happens if i click on it? a single message pops into the stream – basically the final message of the thread. now, what's really important to do here is: if the penultimate message of thread - or rather, the last message that i sent in the thread - is no longer in my field of view, we should assume that i have completely forgotten about this thread. which means just showing me the final message in the thread in the stream (when i click on the thread in the left pane) - is not enough because i don't have the necessary context. we need two π message bubbles rendered: the first is the title and blurb of the thread that gives me the context i need to make sense of it. the second bubble contains the final message that i can respond to. and responding to it is just simply making my own bubble below that and it will naturally just be associated with that thread (using the existing logic / functionality) and things just keep moving forward. <!-- beat:1604 -->

this feels right. this feels really good. ok one more thing, i need to obv be able to archive threads. so we need like a simple – like when i hover over one of the open threads in the left pane, a little trash icon can be shown to the right of it that i can press to archive. ok trash is the wrong icon, just maybe a little "x" button. or fuck that, just put archive in a badge - like a tiny button that says "archive" and that's it. that's enough for now. <!-- beat:1605 -->

and this left pane is always visible. it needs to be always visible to ground my attention in completing tasks and shipping – which is the right counterweight here to my natural thinking. it's pretty clear to me that i can just keep forgetting the past and just naturally keep thinking / exploring endlessly, but that doesn't help me actually create value. gotta ship. and i think i need to bias even more aggressively toward shipping, and i think π (and this product / workflow) can really help me do that. <!-- beat:1606 -->

it's cool how we can use ai to complement us. each of us has our own unique shape of movement, strengths and weaknesses. if we're honest about what we are and what we want, we can use ai to complement us in ways that takes advantage of our strengths and minimizes the harm our weaknesses can create. good. onward. <!-- beat:1608 -->
